# Amendments

Protocol (implementation plan `aart_implementation_plan_v1.md` §7): any change to an S0-frozen interface's **public shape** (a field added/removed/retyped on a `@aart/types` schema, a method signature changed on `AartStore`, `@aart/expr`'s resolver function signature changed) after the `interfaces-frozen-v0` tag requires an entry here — date, discovering session, what changed, why, which other sessions/packages are affected — plus a loud (not silent) backward-compatibility flag if applicable. Internal refactors that don't change a public interface's observable shape don't need an entry.

This file also carries S0's own record of every place the two source documents (`aart_architecture_v1.md`, `aart_product_spec_v2.md`) left a shape underspecified and S0 had to make a concrete, load-bearing choice to freeze something buildable — per this session's own brief: *"If you hit an apparent contradiction on contact, do NOT silently resolve it: pick the amendment-log-supported reading, note it in your report, and record it in AMENDMENTS.md."* None of the entries below are contradictions between the two documents (the multi-round consistency program the docs went through left none on contact) — they are gaps: a method signature never given in TS anywhere, a field name never spelled out, a type never given a literal TS block. Each is flagged here rather than left implicit, so Wave-1 sessions know it was a deliberate S0 choice, not an oversight, and can propose a change through this same protocol if it doesn't fit their needs.

---

## 2026-07-10 — S0 Foundation

### A1 — `AartStore` per-member method signatures designed from scratch
Neither document gives explicit method signatures for any of the 16 `AartStore` sub-interfaces (`WorkflowStore`, `RunStore`, `WaitStore`, ... `StandingApprovalStore`) — spec §28.3 gives one-line prose contracts, architecture §5.3 gives SQL columns. `packages/store/src/types.ts` designs a consistent `get`/`put`/`list` shape per member, plus the specific query methods actually implied by how each store is used elsewhere in both documents (e.g. `WaitStore.listDue(now)` for the scheduler-ticker seam, `SignalStore.findUnconsumedMatch` for the early-arrival check). Affects: every Wave-1 package that reads/writes through `AartStore` (S1, S2, S4, S6, S7). Not a contradiction — a genuine design gap S0 had to fill to ship a buildable interface.

### A2 — `RunStore.hasDedupeKey` / `RunStore.recordDedupeKey` added
Architecture §4.4.2's exactly-once resume dedupe ledger has no store-interface member anywhere in either document — without it, S1's dedupe-check-and-transition pattern would have nothing to call inside `transact()`. Added two methods to `RunStore`; the fs adapter co-locates the dedupe-consumed set inside the same `runs/<runId>.json` file as the `RunRecord` (architecture §5.8's documented mechanism), so staging both through the same `transact()` buffer coalesces them into one atomic write. Affects: S1 (engine, the actual caller).

### A3 — `job_queue`'s fs directory location
Architecture §5.2's fs layout listing does not include a `job_queue` directory, even though `job_queue`'s shape (including `lease_expires_at`/`reclaim_count`) is explicitly S0 scope "from the start" (architecture §4.7/§5.3). Added `.aart/job-queue/<runId>.json`, following the same directory-per-collection convention as every other listed member. Affects: S1 (claim path), S2 (worker liveness — lease renewal, reclaim sweep, graceful shutdown are still S2's to build; S0 only froze the shape and basic get/put/list-claimable primitives, not the race-safety/reclaim logic itself).

### A4 — `EnvironmentStore` dual-keyed by both `id` and `name`
Architecture §5.3's SQL table primary-keys `environments` on `id`; architecture §5's one-line contract states "keyed by name." `DeploymentStore` references environments by `environmentId` (an id, matching the FK), while human-facing lookup (dashboard/CLI) naturally wants lookup by name. Rather than picking one and contradicting the other passage, `EnvironmentStore` exposes both `get(id)` and `getByName(name)`.

### A5 — `StandingApproval.id` added beyond spec §17.6's literal shape
Spec §17.6's `StandingApproval` TS block has no `id` field (it types the policy shape only: `maxRiskTier`/`capabilities`/`grantedBy`/`expiresAt`). Architecture §5's `AartStore` extension states `standingApprovals` is "keyed by id," and architecture §5.3's SQL table backs this with `standing_approvals (id PK, ...)`. Added `id: string` to `@aart/types`' `StandingApprovalSchema` so the type the store actually persists can be keyed as architecture requires. Affects: S4 (writes standing approvals at policy-authoring time).

### A6 — `Workflow.needsReview` / `Workflow.promotionBlocked` added to the canonical type
Spec §23.4 names "block promotion" and "mark workflow version as needs review" as correction outcomes; architecture §5.3 backs them with SQL columns (`workflows.needs_review`, `workflows.promotion_blocked`) but neither document adds them to spec §14.1's literal `Workflow` TS block. Added both as optional booleans directly on `WorkflowSchema` (`packages/types/src/workflow.ts`), following the same flag-and-add pattern architecture already uses for `RunRecord.flag` and `Trigger.dedupeKey`, rather than inventing a parallel store-only query surface for two booleans governance's promotion path (architecture §7.1/§9.4) needs to read at gate-check time. Affects: S4 (`computePromotionState`'s refusal-to-promote check, per architecture's A30 fix), S2 (environment/deployment integration), S6 (writes these fields as correction outcomes).

### A7 — `StepTrace.postHocCorrected` added to the canonical type
Same pattern as A6: architecture §5.3's `step_traces.post_hoc_corrected` column (F5 fix) backs spec §23.4's "update current run output" correction outcome, with no home on spec §19.2's literal `StepTrace` TS block. Added as an optional boolean on `StepTraceSchema`. Affects: S6 (writes it as a correction outcome).

### A8 — Engine-code schema-version tag field named `schemaVersion`
Architecture §4.7 requires "every persisted `WaitCondition`/`RunRecord`" to carry a schema-version tag "distinct from `schema-version.json`'s whole-store migration watermark" but never gives the field an exact name. Named it `schemaVersion` (required, non-optional, on every `WaitCondition` union member and on `RunRecord`) — deliberately distinct from the migration watermark's own `version` key (`schema-version.json: { version: N }`) to avoid the two being confused in code or logs. Affects: S1 (tests resume-time version-compatibility behavior against this exact field, per this session's own DoD note).

### A9 — `store-records.ts` types (`Deployment`, `Environment`, `Schedule`, `PromptRegistryEntry`, `SchemaRegistryEntry`, `PackManifest`, `Correction`, `RejectedTrigger`) derived from architecture §5.3's SQL columns
None of these eight types has a literal spec TS block — spec describes them in prose/YAML examples (§27 deployment modes, §29 scheduling, §16 packs) or, for `Correction`, a JSON example (§23.3). Their Zod shapes in `packages/types/src/store-records.ts` are derived from architecture §5.3's SQL table columns (the one place a complete field list exists for each), camelCased to match this module's convention elsewhere. A few fields were deliberately left open (`z.string()`) rather than closed enums where neither document gives a complete enumerated set — `PackManifest.approvalStatus` (only "unapproved"/"approved" are named in prose, ADR-12/§16.2, with no closed list), `Scorer.kind` (spec §24.3 lists 12 built-in kinds in prose but the type itself is `kind: string`, and a pack/custom scorer shouldn't be rejected by `@aart/types`), `RetryPolicy.backoff`/`retryOn` (architecture §4.2 writes `backoff: "exponential" | ...`, explicitly leaving the rest open) — none of these fabricate enum members the source documents don't actually list.

### A10 — `CapabilityCheck`, `RedactFn`, `BlockImplementation.execute` modeled as plain TypeScript function types, not Zod schemas
This package's DoD asks for "every type in architecture §2.1's inventory... as a Zod schema with a derived `z.infer` type export." These three are function-type dependency-injection seams (a callback signature engine calls, never JSON data anyone validates at a runtime boundary) — modeled as plain `type X = (...) => Y` instead. See `packages/types/src/governance.ts` and `block.ts` for the in-code rationale. Every other type in the §2.1 inventory (~30+ types) is a full Zod schema as instructed.

### A11 — JSON Schema derivation uses Zod 4's native `z.toJSONSchema()`, not the `zod-to-json-schema` package
Architecture ADR-01 names `zod-to-json-schema` as the (parenthetically "or equivalent") derivation mechanism. Verified during this session that the installed `zod@4.4.3` ships a first-party `z.toJSONSchema()` that correctly emits a `oneOf`-with-`const`-per-member shape for a `z.discriminatedUnion` (tested directly against both `WaitCondition` and `Trigger`). Used the native method instead of adding the third-party package as a dependency — same derivation guarantee, one fewer dependency, no risk of the third-party package's internal-representation assumptions drifting from Zod 4's actual internals. `packages/types/src/json-schema.ts`.

### A12 — TypeScript pinned to 5.9.3, not the newly-released 7.0.2
`npm view typescript version` resolved to `7.0.2` (TypeScript 7, the from-scratch native/Go reimplementation of the compiler) at the time this session ran. Deliberately pinned to `5.9.3` (the latest release on the mature 5.x line) instead — for a foundation layer eight parallel Wave-1 sessions build against immediately, the 5.x line's battle-tested ecosystem/tooling compatibility (declaration emit, project-reference/composite-build behavior, third-party `.d.ts` compatibility) outweighs being on the newest major. This is a devDependency choice, not a public-interface shape change, so it's recorded here for visibility rather than as a blocking amendment — bumping to 6.x/7.x later is a routine version bump.

### A13 — `packages/cli`'s `package.json` `name` field is `@team-monet/aart` directly
ADR-18 (A22/A28 fixes) states `@aart/cli` "is renamed/published as `@team-monet/aart`" with no separate rename mechanism described anywhere (no `publishConfig.name` override named in either document). Read this literally: `packages/cli/package.json`'s `name` field is `"@team-monet/aart"` from the start, not `"@aart/cli"` — the directory is `packages/cli`, the package name is not `@aart/*` at all, matching how every `npx @team-monet/aart ...` example throughout both documents already invokes it. It is the one package not marked `private` (ADR-18/A28).

### A14 — `examples/` subdirectories not pre-created
Implementation plan §2 says S0 "just creates the directory + a README stub." Read as: create `examples/` itself plus `examples/README.md`, but not either flagship example's own subdirectory (domain content since removed, A70) — S1 authors the guarded-loop fragment into the former (creating it as part of that work), S9 authors the latter "from scratch" (P19 amendment). Noted here since the plan's wording is compatible with either reading.

### A15 — pnpm build-script approval recorded in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`
`isolated-vm` is a native addon; pnpm 10's default security posture ignores its install script (`node-gyp rebuild`) unless explicitly approved. Ran `pnpm approve-builds --all`, which pnpm persisted as `allowBuilds: { isolated-vm: true }` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (committed) — without this, every fresh `pnpm install` (Wave-1 sessions, CI) would silently skip building `isolated-vm`'s native binding and the isolated-vm smoke test (and, later, S1's sandbox) would fail with a missing-binary error, not an obviously-native-addon-related one. Not an interface change; recorded here because it's exactly the kind of platform friction this session's smoke tests exist to surface, and the fix needed to be committed, not just applied locally once.

## 2026-07-10 — S1 Engine + durable execution

### A16 — `Workflow.concurrency` added to the canonical type
Spec §30.1 shows `concurrency: { key: "{{ inputs.caseId }}", policy: "queue" }` as a "Per workflow" YAML example, and architecture §4.3 fully designs the runtime enforcement semantics for all four `ConcurrencyPolicy` values (`queue`/`cancel_existing`/`reject_new`/`allow`) against exactly this shape ("the engine's trigger-intake path resolves the concurrency key and checks the store...") — but neither document ever adds a field for it to spec §14.1's literal `Workflow` TS block, nor does S0's own `workflow.ts` (which otherwise mirrors every other architecture-flagged addition — `needsReview`/`promotionBlocked`, A6 above). Without a home on the canonical type, this session's concurrency-policy DoD item (implementation plan S1: "Concurrency policies... tested against a fixture with a `{{ }}`-resolved concurrency key") has no field to read a workflow's declared policy from at all — a genuine blocking gap, not a style preference.

Added `concurrency?: { key: string; policy: ConcurrencyPolicy }` to `WorkflowSchema` (`packages/types/src/workflow.ts`), following the exact same additive flag-and-add pattern already established by A6 (`needsReview`/`promotionBlocked`) and by `RunRecord.flag`/`Trigger.dedupeKey` elsewhere in this file — optional, additive, no existing field touched or retyped. `key` is a `{{ }}` expression string (resolved against `inputs.*` at trigger time, architecture §4.3); `policy` reuses the already-frozen `ConcurrencyPolicySchema` (governance.ts) rather than a new enum. Round-trip + rejection tests added to `workflow.test.ts`.

**Backward compatibility:** purely additive (a new optional field) — every previously-valid `Workflow` object remains valid; no other Wave-1 package's existing code is affected. Flagged here per the amendment protocol's "no silent absorption" rule regardless, since it changes `@aart/types`' public shape post-freeze.

**Affects:** `@aart/engine` (this session, the sole current consumer — `packages/engine/src/concurrency.ts`). Also relevant to S5 (`@aart/mcp`'s YAML `uses`/`with` compiler, §14.2/§10.3) and S4 (governance's approval-summary/trust-surface-completeness test, ADR-17 — a new execution-affecting `Workflow` field should render in the approval summary the same as any other) if/when either session's own work touches workflow-level fields; flagged here so both can pick it up without re-discovering the gap themselves.

## 2026-07-10 — S2 Server

**Renumbering note (S9 integration, 2026-07-10):** S1 and S2 branched concurrently from `interfaces-frozen-v0` and each independently continued this log from A16. S1 merged first (A16 above); S2's original A16-A19 are renumbered A17-A20 below, content and attribution unchanged. See S9's own consolidated renumbering entry near the end of this file for the full cross-branch mapping (S1, S2, S4 all independently started at A16).

### A17 — SQLite adapter built on Node's built-in `node:sqlite`, not `better-sqlite3`/`sqlite3`
Neither document names a specific SQLite binding for architecture §5.1's "SQLite | Single-node production" adapter. S2 (`packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/**`, the declared carve-out per implementation plan §3's preamble) uses Node's built-in `node:sqlite` (`DatabaseSync`) rather than adding `better-sqlite3` (or `sqlite3`) as a new dependency. Rationale: (1) the workspace already carries one native-addon platform-friction risk (`isolated-vm`, implementation plan Risk 3) — `better-sqlite3` is ALSO a native addon requiring a native build step, i.e. a second instance of exactly the risk class S0's smoke tests exist to surface, whereas `node:sqlite` ships inside Node itself (verified present and functional on the installed `node@22.22.2`, matching this workspace's `engines.node: ">=22"` floor — Node's sqlite support landed at 22.5); (2) zero new dependency footprint, consistent with this codebase's existing preference for built-ins over third-party packages where one suffices (A11's `z.toJSONSchema()` choice is the same pattern). `node:sqlite` currently logs a one-line `ExperimentalWarning: SQLite is an experimental feature` on first use — functionally inert (does not affect behavior or exit codes), left as-is rather than suppressed, so it stays visible to whoever operates this in production. `node:sqlite`'s `DatabaseSync`/`StatementSync` are synchronous, same programming model as `better-sqlite3` — every `AartStore` method built on top still returns a `Promise`, so this is invisible to callers either way. Not a blocking amendment (this is an implementation choice inside S2's own carve-out, not a change to any frozen interface's public shape) — recorded for visibility per A11/A15's established pattern, since a future session (S9, if it later needs to touch this adapter) or reviewer should know this was a deliberate choice, not an oversight.

### A18 — SQLite adapter stores `RunRecord.trace`/`waits`/`artifacts` as columns on `runs`, not architecture §5.3's literal separate `step_traces` table
Architecture §5.3's literal `runs` table has no column for `trace`/`waits`/`artifacts` — its design implies reconstructing `RunRecord.trace` via a join against a separate `step_traces` table. Two problems with implementing that literally: (1) `RunStore`'s frozen interface (`packages/store/src/types.ts`, S0-owned, outside this carve-out) exposes trace/waits/artifacts *only* as fields embedded in the `RunRecord` `get()`/`put()` round-trip — no `AartStore` member exposes step-trace-level SQL access independent of the parent `RunRecord`, so a separate table would have no reader; (2) reconstructing `RunRecord.waits[]` specifically from architecture's own `waits` table (this adapter's `WaitStore`) would be actively wrong — that table holds only the *current outstanding* wait per `(run_id, step_id)`, deleted on resolve (architecture §5.6/the `WaitStore.delete` contract), while `RunRecord.waits` is an ever-growing historical array (architecture §4.4 step 4: "RunRecord.waits array gets this WaitCondition appended") — a resolved-and-deleted wait can never be recovered by joining against the live `waits` table. Given S0's conformance suite requires exact round-trip fidelity (`toEqual` deep equality) and no interface consumer needs step-trace-level SQL querying today, `runs.trace_json`/`waits_json`/`artifacts_json` hold these arrays directly as the source of truth; architecture's literal `step_traces` table is left as documented future work (a real SQL-queryable table with no current caller and real dual-write drift risk is not built speculatively). `runs.schema_version` is also added beyond architecture §5.3's literal column list — a gap-fill, not a contradiction: `RunRecord.schemaVersion` (architecture §4.7/A8) was added to the frozen type after §5.3's column list was last written, so the SQL listing was never updated for a field the type already requires. Affects: S9 if it later builds a step-trace-level query surface — the physical layout to extend or supersede is documented in `packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/schema.ts`'s module doc comment.

### A19 — `packages/store`'s `package.json` does not yet expose the SQLite adapter to sibling packages — ACTION NEEDED, outside this session's carve-out
S2's carve-out is `packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/**` only — it does not extend to `packages/store/package.json` or `packages/store/src/index.ts` (both S0-owned). Those two files currently export only `"."` (the fs-adapter-backed main entry point); there is no subpath export for `adapters/sqlite/**`. Consequence, verified directly: no OTHER package in this workspace — including `@aart/server` itself — can currently `import { createSqliteStore } from "@aart/store"` or any subpath thereof; Node/TypeScript's `exports`-map resolution refuses any path not explicitly listed. **This session's own `@aart/server` code and tests deliberately do NOT depend on the SQLite adapter** (composition is via a caller-supplied `AartStore` — see SEAMS.md's `SharedRuntimeConfig.store` — and this package's own tests use `createFsStore`, already properly exported) precisely because of this gap, so it did not block S2's own DoD. It DOES block whoever eventually wires `aart server --store=sqlite:<path>` (most likely `@aart/cli`, S5, or S9's integration pass) from reaching the adapter at all. **Proposed fix** (one of, applied at the source per the amendment protocol's "gets it applied at the source" step — this session cannot apply it directly): add a `"./sqlite"` entry to `packages/store/package.json`'s `exports` map pointing at `dist/adapters/sqlite/index.d.ts`/`.js` (matching the existing `"."` entry's shape), OR re-export the sqlite adapter's public surface (`createSqliteStore`, `openSqliteStore`, `SqliteStoreHandle`, `CreateSqliteStoreOptions`) from `packages/store/src/index.ts` directly. Either is a small, low-risk, backward-compatible addition (adds a subpath, changes nothing existing). Affects: S5 (CLI's `aart server`/`aart worker` store-selection wiring), S9 (integration pass — this is exactly the kind of seam-that-didn't-converge Risk 5 names).

**S9 resolution (2026-07-10): RESOLVED.** Added `"./sqlite"` to `packages/store/package.json`'s `exports` map (pointing at `dist/adapters/sqlite/index.{d.ts,js}`, mirroring the existing `"."` entry) — see S9's own integration-fix commit. `@aart/store/sqlite` now resolves for any workspace consumer; `@aart/cli`'s `--store=sqlite:<path>` wiring uses it (Phase 2 item 9).

### A20 — Trigger-intake dedupe-key check is a linear scan, not an indexed lookup — scaling limitation, proposed fix outside this session's carve-out
Architecture §6.1's FLAGGED DIVERGENCE requires intake to check `Trigger.dedupeKey` "against previously-seen keys for that trigger/deployment before run creation." No `AartStore` member (the frozen 16 + job_queue/idempotency_ledger) persists a delivery-identity ledger independent of the `RunRecord`s a delivery produced — `packages/store/src/types.ts` is S0-owned, outside this session's carve-out, so this session could not add one itself. `packages/server/src/triggers/intake.ts`'s `findRunByDedupeKey` instead scans `store.runs.list({ workflowId })` (bounded to one workflow, not the whole store) and checks each run's own embedded `trigger.dedupeKey` for a match — correct, but O(n) in that workflow's run count rather than an indexed lookup, and it only catches a redelivery of a trigger that already produced a `RunRecord` (a delivery rejected for another reason first — e.g. `bad_hmac` — has no `RunRecord` to scan, so an immediately-following genuine redelivery with the same `dedupeKey` would not be caught by this check alone, though `rejected_triggers`' own audit trail still records both attempts individually). **Proposed fix**, for whoever has write access to `packages/store/src/types.ts` (S9, or a coordinator-approved amendment): add a small dedicated member, e.g. `RunStore.findByDedupeKey(workflowId, dedupeKey): Promise<RunRecord | undefined>` (adapter-implementable as an indexed lookup — SQL: `WHERE workflow_id = ? AND json_extract(trigger_json, '$.dedupeKey') = ?`, or a small secondary index) — S2's `findRunByDedupeKey` should switch to calling it once it exists, deleting the interim linear-scan implementation. Affects: S9 (real production throughput at scale), any session touching `@aart/store`'s frozen interface next.

**S9 resolution (2026-07-10): DEFERRED, accepted for v1.** `RunStore` is already a 16-member frozen interface with real adapters (fs, sqlite) built against it; adding `findByDedupeKey` now is a real interface-shape change this late in the build, for a bound (one workflow's run count) that is not a demonstrated production problem yet. Accepted as a documented v1 scaling limitation rather than an interface amendment — `intake.ts`'s `findRunByDedupeKey` now carries an inline comment pointing back to this entry. Revisit if/when a workflow's per-workflow run count is large enough for the linear scan to matter in practice.

## 2026-07-10 — S4 Governance

**Renumbering note (S9 integration, 2026-07-10):** S4 also branched concurrently from `interfaces-frozen-v0` and independently continued this log from A16, unaware of S1's/S2's own A16+ additions. Renumbered S4's original A16-A21 to A21-A26 below, content and attribution unchanged.

None of the entries below changes an S0-frozen interface's public shape (this session never touched `packages/types/**` or `packages/store/**`) — continuing this file's broader established use (per S0's own framing above: "gaps... left underspecified... a concrete, load-bearing choice") for governance-owned design gaps and one genuine spec-internal inconsistency this session's own test-writing surfaced. Flagged here, not just in code comments, so a later session (S9 in particular) can find them in one place.

### A21 — `computeApprovalState`: an empty `requiredGatesForMode` never vacuously auto-approves
Architecture §7.3's explicit mode->required-gates mapping states dev mode "requires nothing" (an empty array) in the same breath as "approval never auto-flips... dev is meant for throwaway iteration, not promotion." Read literally, "every gate in an empty set has passed" is vacuously true, which would auto-approve every dev-mode workflow immediately — directly contradicting "never auto-flips" one sentence later. `computeApprovalState(gates, requiredGatesForMode)` (`packages/governance/src/approval.ts`) resolves this by treating an empty `requiredGatesForMode` as never-satisfiable rather than vacuously satisfied. This is the only `requiredGatesForMode` value any of the four documented trust modes actually supplies (dev's — see `REQUIRED_GATES_BY_MODE`, `gates.ts`), so the rule is dev-mode-specific in practice without `computeApprovalState` needing a mode parameter to know it. Tested explicitly in `approval.test.ts` ("dev NEVER auto-flips to approved, even with every gate passed").

### A22 — Capability risk table gaps (`queue`, `db.read`) interpolated to Medium
Spec §31.0's capability taxonomy names 10 base capabilities; §31.1's risk table (the very next subsection) assigns an explicit risk tier to only 8 of them — `queue` and `db.read` are named in the taxonomy but never given a risk-table row (not a contradiction between the two documents, a silent gap within the SAME document). `riskForCapability` (`packages/governance/src/capability.ts`) interpolates both to Medium, by analogy by the table's own file.read (Low-medium) / file.write (Medium) pair, which establishes a one-tier "read is safer than write" delta within one resource family: db.read sits one tier below db.write's High -> Medium; queue is an external-system interaction comparable in kind to http/browser/llm (all Medium), not merely local like file.read. Any capability string outside the documented taxonomy entirely defaults to High (fail-closed, not fail-open) — see the same function. Affects: S1/S9 (capability closure risk is what the approval summary and `getGrantedCapabilities` both key off).

### A23 — `ApprovalTask`'s `runId`/`stepId` sentinel convention for workflow-VERSION-level decisions
`ApprovalTask`'s frozen shape (spec §13.5) is `runId`/`stepId`-keyed for a per-run wait-type approval (a `human.approval` step's decision). Architecture §7.2 requires PR-merge ingestion to write an `ApprovalTask` "going through the exact same... write path as a CLI/dashboard decision" — but a PR-merge (or a standing-approval auto-pass) decides a workflow VERSION's promotion eligibility (the `humanReview` gate) before that version has necessarily ever run; there is no real run/step to key off. Neither source document resolves this identity gap. `workflowVersionApprovalSubject(workflowId, workflowVersion)` (`packages/governance/src/approval-tasks.ts`) resolves it with a documented sentinel pair: `runId: "workflow-version:<workflowId>@<workflowVersion>"`, `stepId: "__gate:humanReview__"` — used consistently by every workflow-version-level `ApprovalTask` writer in this package (`recordPrMergeApproval`, `recordStandingApprovalDecision`). A genuine per-run `human.approval` wait's `ApprovalTask` always carries real `runId`/`stepId` from its own `RunRecord`/`WorkflowStep` and never goes through this helper. Affects: S2 (github trigger adapter calling `recordPrMergeApproval`), S8 (dashboard rendering approval tasks — should recognize this sentinel shape as "a version-level decision," not a malformed run reference).

**S9 flag (2026-07-10, pending reconciliation):** S5 independently filled the identical type gap for MCP/CLI-originated approval decisions with a DIFFERENT sentinel encoding (`SEAMS.md`'s S5 entry, "Load-bearing interpretive decisions": `runId: "version-review:<workflowId>@<workflowVersion>"`, `stepId: "humanReview"`). Two non-interoperable encodings of the same workflow-version-decision concept currently coexist. Tracked as this session's Phase 2 reconciliation item 2 — resolution recorded in a follow-up entry once decided (not yet resolved as of this merge commit).

**S9 resolution (2026-07-11, reconciliation ledger item 1): governance's convention wins.** Confirmed via independent analyst review before implementing (role contract requires delegating this kind of cross-package interface decision rather than deciding solo). Governance owns the underlying approval-state computation and the `ApprovalTask` write path itself, so it owns this identity convention too; `@aart/mcp`'s `version-review:`/`humanReview` encoding was this session's own documented PROVISIONAL fill, explicitly self-flagged in its own doc comment as revisable ("if a cleaner mechanism emerges once S4's real approval-task flows are wired end to end, this sentinel convention should be revisited — it's this session's own fill for a genuine type-shape gap, not a frozen contract"). Governance's `__gate:humanReview__` stepId is also a more robust sentinel shape than the alternative's bare `"humanReview"` — the alternative could theoretically collide with a real `WorkflowStep.id` literally named `humanReview`; governance's double-underscore-and-colon form cannot.

Resolution: added `decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject` to `packages/governance/src/approval-tasks.ts` (governance previously published only the encode half); extended `@aart/mcp`'s `GovernancePort`/`createStubGovernance` with both `workflowVersionApprovalSubject`/`decodeWorkflowVersionApprovalSubject` plus `writeApprovalDecision`; `mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts`'s `requestApprovalHandler`/`approveHandler` now call through `ctx.governance` instead of maintaining their own `VERSION_REVIEW_PREFIX`/`encodeVersionReviewRunId`/`decodeVersionReviewRunId` (all three deleted).

**Also fixed a real, previously-silent bug found during this reconciliation:** `@aart/dashboard`'s `views/approvals.ts`'s `decideApprovalAction` called `resumeApproval` UNCONDITIONALLY for every decided task, including workflow-version-level ones — against a sentinel `runId` the real `Engine.resumeApproval` finds no matching wait and returns `{kind:"unmatched"}` (a safe no-op by design), so a dashboard operator's version-level approval decision was silently swallowed prior to this fix: the `ApprovalTask` record updated, but `Workflow.gates.humanReview`/`approval` never did, with no error surfaced anywhere. Fixed by decoding the runId first and, when it decodes, applying the same gate-update logic `@aart/mcp`'s `applyVersionReviewDecision` uses instead of `resumeApproval`. The approval-queue page's task-to-run link is also fixed (previously linked every task to `/runs/<runId>`, a dead link for a sentinel runId).

**Also fixed, same pass — a redaction-bypass finding:** `mcp/src/handlers/governance.ts`'s `requestApprovalHandler`/`approveHandler` wrote `ApprovalTask` rows via `store.approvals.put(...)` directly, bypassing `@aart/governance`'s `writeApprovalDecision` — the one path architecture §7.9's redaction-chokepoint diagram names explicitly for "approval decision" (a task's free-form `decision` field can echo back arbitrary data). Both handlers now route through `ctx.governance.writeApprovalDecision`.

**Found, NOT fixed in this pass — flagged, not silently absorbed (plan's own instruction: "if S9 discovers a Wave-1 package needs substantial rework... flag it explicitly rather than quietly absorbing a redesign into this session's scope"):** `packages/server/src/triggers/adapters.ts`'s `ingestGithubPrMergeApproval` does **not** call `recordPrMergeApproval` as this amendment's original text (and S2's own SEAMS.md) assumed — it is a third, independent write path (a pending-approval lookup + an inline `store.approvals.put`, bypassing both the sentinel convention AND redaction). It is fed by an injected `resolveGithubApprovalTarget` callback (`ServerConfig.resolveGithubApprovalTarget`) that has **no real implementation anywhere in this codebase** — only a literal test fixture (`server/src/http/server.test.ts`) supplies one. The github PR-merge-as-approval path is therefore not reachable end-to-end today, independent of this reconciliation. This is real, standalone rework (not a surgical seam patch) — tracked for a follow-up session, not fixed here.

Tests: `packages/mcp/src/handlers/governance.test.ts` (sentinel format), `packages/dashboard/src/views/approvals.test.ts` (the bug fix itself, the dead-link fix, and a false-positive-proof for a genuine per-run task). Full suite: 203 files / 1999 tests passing.

### A24 — Semantic risk diff: the spec's own §17.4 prose example is not mechanically reproducible under §31.1's formal capability taxonomy
Found while building the checkout-smoke v0.1.0->v0.2.0 test fixture spec §17.4 itself provides (`packages/governance/src/risk-diff.test.ts`). The spec's illustrative prose claims adding a `browser.click` submit step increases risk "Low -> Medium." But §31.0's own capability model is explicit that capabilities are "coarse grants — not one capability per block": every `browser.*` block (goto/click/screenshot/etc.) declares the SAME `browser` capability. A checkout-smoke workflow that already opens a browser and reads a page (as the v0.1.0 example itself does, via `browser.goto`/`web.read`) has ALREADY put `browser` (Medium, §31.1) in its capability closure before v0.2.0 adds anything — so a second `browser.*` step cannot, under the formal taxonomy this same architecture defines, produce a NEW capability or a risk-tier delta. This is a spec-internal inconsistency between a hand-written narrative example and the formal rule sitting one section away, not a bug in this implementation — documented in `risk-diff.test.ts` rather than silently forcing a matching (but mechanically false) test assertion. `semanticRiskDiff`'s risk-tier-delta mechanism is separately verified correct against a fixture where the closure genuinely gains a new capability (the same test file, "risk-tier delta is a genuine ceiling-function recompute"). Flagged for whoever owns the spec's own example prose, should it ever be revised — the fix would be picking a genuinely-new-capability example (e.g. adding a `command.run` verification step) rather than a second `browser.*` step.

### A25 — Validation class 2: "no direct self-reference" is unconditional, independent of the "unguarded cycle" guard rule
Spec §18.2 lists "no direct self-reference" and "cycles must declare exit guards (`maxIterations`/`until`)" as two separate bullets. Neither source document's own worked example (architecture §18.2's guarded-renewal-cycle fixture — domain content since removed, A70) demonstrates a true self-loop — `rescan.next` targets `recheck_wait`, a DIFFERENT earlier step, never itself — so nothing shows a guarded self-loop being accepted. `validateReferences` (`packages/governance/src/validation/reference.ts`) treats these as two independent rules: `step.next === step.id` is ALWAYS an error regardless of any guard declared; any OTHER back-edge (targeting a different step at or before its own array index) requires the DECLARING step — not the target — to carry `maxIterations`/`until`, matching the worked example exactly (`rescan`, the step WITH `next`, is the one that declares the guard). Tested in `reference.test.ts`, including the literal spec worked example as a passing fixture. (Identifiers updated post-hoc to match this entry's own A70 rename — see A70's own follow-up note.)

### A26 — `getGrantedCapabilities` and `PromotionRecord` are governance's own design fill, not frozen shapes
Cross-referenced in `SEAMS.md` (not duplicated in full here): neither `getGrantedCapabilities` (the granted-set policy query architecture §4.6's dispatch pseudocode calls `governance.getGrantedCapabilities(workflowVersion, environment)`) nor `PromotionRecord`'s exact field shape (architecture §7.1: "its exact field shape is ADR-07's/S2's to finalize") is given a fixed signature by either source document, unlike `computeApprovalState`/`computePromotionState`/`checkCapability`/`redactRecord`, which are. Both are implemented with a reasonable, documented shape open to revision by S1/S2/S9 without a further amendment, since nothing outside this package's own tests currently depends on their exact signature.

**S9 flag (2026-07-10, pending reconciliation):** S2 independently built its own `PromotionRecord`/`computePromotionState`/`computeApprovalState` mirror (`packages/server/src/promotion.ts`, documented in S2's own `SEAMS.md` entry as "the seam S4's real governance package must satisfy (or S9 must adapt)") against this exact documented-fill shape. Whether the two shapes are structurally identical, and how S2's composition root switches from its mirror to this package's real exports, is this session's Phase 2 reconciliation item 4 — not yet verified or resolved as of this merge commit.

**S9 resolution (2026-07-11, reconciliation ledger item 2): governance's `PromotionRecord` shape ratified as canonical — NOT structurally identical to S2's mirror, verified by direct comparison, not assumed.** S2's mirror carried `environmentId`/`environmentName`/`promoted`/`globalApproval`/`computedAt` (a 5-field shape with a timestamp and a split id/name); governance's real shape is `environment`/`promoted`/`globalApproval`/`requiredGates`/`unmetGates` (also 5 fields, but `environment` is a single id string, no timestamp, and it separately surfaces which gates were unmet). `@aart/dashboard`'s own independent third mirror (`deps.ts`) had ALREADY converged on governance's exact shape before this reconciliation — corroborating evidence that governance's shape, not S2's, is the natural one. Confirmed via independent analyst review before implementing (role contract requires delegating this kind of cross-package interface decision rather than deciding solo) that dropping S2's `environmentId`/`environmentName`/`computedAt` loses nothing observable: neither is persisted into `Deployment` (which only ever stores `environmentId`) nor asserted on by any existing test.

Resolution: `packages/server/src/promotion.ts` deleted its local `computeApprovalState`/`PromotionRecord`/`computePromotionState`/`REQUIRED_GATES_BY_TRUST_MODE` and now imports+re-exports the real functions from `@aart/governance` (new dependency added to `packages/server/package.json`). `promoteWorkflowVersionToEnvironment` keeps its own workflow/environment fetch and `Deployment` create-or-refresh unchanged; its eligibility computation now calls governance's real `evaluatePromotionForEnvironment` — the explicit `workflow.promotionBlocked` check stays exactly where it was (before the environment fetch), preserving the original error-priority ordering (`blocked_by_promotion_block` beats `environment_not_found`) even though the wrapper performs the identical check internally (belt-and-suspenders, not dead logic). `@aart/dashboard`'s `deps.ts`/`stub-deps.ts` deleted their own three local mirrors (`computeApprovalState`, `evaluatePromotionForEnvironment`, `makePromoteWorkflowVersionToEnvironment`) and now bind directly to `@aart/governance`'s and `@aart/server`'s real exports (`@aart/governance`/`@aart/server` added as new dashboard dependencies) — including one real, load-bearing type-shape fix: governance's `evaluatePromotionForEnvironment` returns `{blocked:true;reason;environment}|{blocked:false;record}`, NOT dashboard's former locally-invented `{kind:"blocked"}|{kind:"evaluated",record}` — grep-verified before the swap that no view/action in the dashboard package pattern-matched on the old shape, so this was a value-source swap with no call-site rewrite needed beyond the dashboard's own 3 direct unit tests of `evaluatePromotionForEnvironment` itself. Tests: `packages/server/src/promotion.test.ts` and `packages/dashboard/src/stub-deps.test.ts` updated for the new signatures/shapes; full suite green (203 files / 1996 tests).

## 2026-07-11 — S9 Integration — critical finding, not one of the pre-enumerated reconciliation ledger items

### A27 — `@aart/engine`'s `createTrackingSecretResolver` tracked the resolved secret's NAME, not its VALUE — redaction was silently a no-op end-to-end

**Severity: highest-priority correctness/security finding of the entire integration pass** — higher than the sentinel collision (A23), because this one meant the system's core security promise (ADR-10, "the single redaction chokepoint") was not actually functioning, in a way NO Wave-1 package's own test suite could have caught (each side's tests independently self-consistent, but wrong relative to each other's real contract) — discovered only by a genuine end-to-end test against the REAL merged `@aart/governance` `redactRecord`, exactly the class of bug a hardening wave's integration testing exists to catch that per-package testing against mocks structurally cannot.

**The bug:** `@aart/types`' frozen `RedactFn` contract, and `@aart/governance`'s real `redactRecord` (its only real implementation, `packages/governance/src/redact.ts`) — both explicitly documented, in-code, that the `resolvedSecretRefs: ReadonlySet<string>` parameter holds resolved secret **VALUES** ("the set's members are used directly as search values, which is only executable if they ARE the values, not just their symbolic names" — `redact.ts`'s own doc comment; architecture §7.9: "replaces any occurrence of that literal resolved value"). `@aart/engine`'s `createTrackingSecretResolver` (`packages/engine/src/redaction.ts`) — the ONE place in the engine that populates this set, threaded through every persist call site in the package (run-lifecycle.ts/step-executor.ts/wait-machine.ts, all via `applyRedaction`) — instead did `resolvedRefs.add(name)`, tracking the symbolic secret NAME (e.g. `"API_KEY"`) that was looked up, not the VALUE the resolver actually returned (e.g. a real API key). `redactRecord` would then scan persisted records for literal occurrences of `"API_KEY"` — a string that essentially never coincidentally appears in real execution output — instead of the real secret value, so **no real secret value was ever actually redacted from a persisted `RunRecord`/`StepTrace`/`ExecutionSnapshot` in the real merged system**, despite every one of `@aart/engine`'s own pre-integration tests passing.

**Why S1's own tests didn't catch it:** `@aart/engine`'s own `redaction.test.ts`/`step-executor.test.ts` tests were built self-consistently around the SAME (wrong) mental model — asserting `resolvedSecretRefs.has("API_KEY")` (the name) as correct/expected, and one test's own mock redactor derived its search value FROM the tracked name (`` `secret-value-for-${ref}` ``) specifically to round-trip against a same-shaped mock `resolveSecret`, which worked precisely because the mock happened to make name and value derivable from each other — never exercising `@aart/governance`'s actual value-based contract. `@aart/governance`'s own tests only ever fed `redactRecord` values directly (never through the engine's resolver-tracking mechanism), so they never observed the mismatch either. Each package was internally correct against its own understanding and its own tests; the two understandings simply didn't match — a seam-convergence failure by definition, and precisely why S9's DoD frames the whole hardening wave as verifying "every cross-session interface seam... end-to-end, not just per-package."

**How it was found:** `packages/mcp/src/real-context.test.ts`'s "redaction genuinely runs end-to-end" test — built during S9's composition-root wiring (reconciliation ledger items 3/4/5/11) — constructs a REAL `@aart/engine` `Engine` wired to the REAL `@aart/governance` `redactRecord` (not either package's own mocks) and asserts a resolved secret value never reaches the persisted store. It failed on first run; the persisted `RunRecord`'s trace showed the raw secret value `"super-secret-value"` completely unredacted, while the workflow DEFINITION's unrelated `{{ secrets.API_KEY }}` template text had been mangled instead (`"{{ secrets.[REDACTED:secret-1] }}"`) — the literal string `"API_KEY"` matching inside the template text itself, nowhere near an actual secret.

**Fix:** `packages/engine/src/redaction.ts`'s `createTrackingSecretResolver` now does `if (typeof value === "string") resolvedRefs.add(value)` — tracks the resolved VALUE (guarding against `undefined`, since `Set<string>` can't hold it and `redactRecord` has no defined behavior for a non-string search value). No other file in the engine needed to change — every consumer already threads the SAME set through to `applyRedaction`/`config.redact` correctly; the bug was isolated to this one function's one line. Confirmed via `grep` that no other package has a similar tracking-resolver mechanism (this pattern is `@aart/engine`-only).

**Test fallout, all fixed in the same pass (the bug being fixed is exactly why these needed updating — they encoded the wrong behavior as "expected"):** `packages/engine/src/redaction.test.ts` (`createTrackingSecretResolver` describe block — now asserts VALUES are tracked, plus a new test for the `undefined`-value guard), `packages/engine/src/step-executor.test.ts` (three tests: the two pre-existing "redaction routing" tests, plus this same session's own newly-added `recordLlmCall`-redaction test from reconciliation ledger item 6 — which inherited the SAME buggy assumption when written, since it was modeled on the pre-existing tests before this bug was known).

**Residual risk / what this does NOT cover:** this fix makes redaction correct going forward; it does not retroactively re-redact anything already persisted by a pre-fix run (not applicable here — no production data exists yet, this is pre-publish). Confirmed no other package independently reimplements this tracking pattern. Recommend the Phase 3 security pass explicitly re-verify this exact mechanism adversarially (not just via this regression test) given its severity.

Tests: `packages/mcp/src/real-context.test.ts` (the test that found it, now passing), `packages/engine/src/redaction.test.ts`, `packages/engine/src/step-executor.test.ts`. Full suite run 3 times consecutively post-fix to rule out non-determinism (a security-critical fix warrants more than one green run): 204 files / 2005 tests, stable all 3 runs.

## 2026-07-11 — S9 Phase 3 (flagship example workflow authoring) — second unplanned discovery

### A28 — `@aart/blocks-core`'s `human.correct` claimed to pause execution; it never did (architecture §4.4's wait-block-id list is exhaustive, and doesn't include it)

**Found while authoring the flagship example workflow's full flow** (spec §36.1's "human approval/correction" step; domain content since removed, A70) — `human.correct` looked like the obviously-correct block for a step literally named "correction," so the E2E test harness was written to use it first. Its wait never fired: the worker process reached the step, ran straight through it, and landed on the SECOND human-in-the-loop gate without ever pausing at the first.

**Root cause:** `packages/blocks-core/src/human/correct.ts`'s (S3) original doc comment and output shape claimed `human.correct` was "wait-shaped, reusing the SAME `WaitCondition{type:'approval'}` mechanism `human.approval` uses... this block genuinely pauses the run" — its `execute()` constructed a `{type:"approval", taskId, timeout, title, description, currentValue}` object, structurally resembling the real `WaitCondition` shape. But `@aart/engine`'s wait-dispatch mechanism (S1, `packages/engine/src/wait/wait-blocks.ts`'s `WAIT_BLOCK_IDS`) recognizes exactly 7 block ids as wait-triggering — a **verbatim, exhaustive enumeration copied directly from architecture §4.4's own wait-lifecycle diagram**: "Step dispatches to a wait-type block (`wait.for_signal` / `wait.until` / `wait.for_webhook` / `wait.for_external_job` / `wait.for_queue` / `wait.manual` / `human.approval`)" — confirmed by direct re-read of the architecture document itself, not just of S1's own (also-correct) test asserting `isWaitBlockId("human.correct") === false`. `human.correct` was never on that list, on either side: not in the architecture's own enumeration, and not (as an oversight) added to the engine's implementation of it. A step using `human.correct` therefore dispatches through the engine's ORDINARY (non-wait) execution path — `execute()` runs to completion immediately, its `type:"approval"`-shaped output is treated as a plain completed-step output nothing further interprets, and the run continues straight past it. `human.review`'s own doc comment (`packages/blocks-core/src/human/review.ts`) already candidly notes this exact ambiguity was known at authoring time: "human.review and human.correct are both underspecified beyond their names in spec §15.3 — no per-block schema is given there for either" — `human.review` was correctly built as a synchronous marker; `human.correct`, considered alongside it, was given a materially different (and, per the frozen architecture, incorrect) wait-shaped treatment.

**Why this wasn't caught earlier:** `human.correct.test.ts` (blocks-core's own suite) only ever asserted the block's `execute()` returns the documented (wait-mimicking) shape — internally consistent with its own (wrong) premise, never exercised against the real engine's dispatch logic. `wait-blocks.test.ts` (engine's own suite) correctly asserts `human.correct` is NOT a recognized wait-block id — internally consistent with the real architecture, but nothing cross-checked that assertion against `human.correct`'s own claimed behavior on the other side. Two internally-correct-in-isolation Wave-1 packages, never merged and executed together until now — the same seam-convergence shape as A27's redaction finding, on a different seam.

**Fix:** `packages/blocks-core/src/human/correct.ts` rewritten to match its ACTUAL (synchronous) behavior rather than perpetuate a misleading one — mirrors `human.review`'s honest pattern exactly. Output shape changed from `{type:"approval", taskId, timeout, title, description, currentValue}` to `{correctionRequested: true, title, description, currentValue}` — no `type`/`taskId`/`timeout` fields that could be mistaken for a real `WaitCondition`. Doc comment now states plainly: "does NOT pause the run... use `human.approval` instead if the workflow must wait for a decision." `packages/blocks-core/src/human/correct.test.ts` rewritten for the new shape, including an explicit `not.toHaveProperty("type"/"taskId")` regression guard against reintroducing the wait-mimicking shape. `packages/blocks-core/src/human/index.ts` and `catalog.test.ts`'s doc comments corrected (both previously stated "human.correct is wait-shaped"). `packages/engine/src/wait/wait-blocks.ts`/`wait-blocks.test.ts` needed NO change — they were already correct, faithfully implementing the frozen architecture; this was exclusively a blocks-core-side fix.

**The flagship example workflow** (this same session, Phase 3) uses `human.approval` for BOTH human-in-the-loop gates as a result (not `human.correct` for the first one, despite spec §36.1's step being named "human approval/correction") — "correction" is expressed through that step's own `title`/`description` text, since no distinct engine mechanism for it exists. Documented in that example's own README/YAML header comments at the time (the example itself, and those docs, were later removed — domain content since removed, A70), not silently routed around.

**Residual risk:** any workflow authored BEFORE this fix that used `human.correct` expecting it to pause would have silently NOT paused — a real correctness gap for whoever hit it, now closed for anything authored going forward. No production workflows exist yet (pre-publish), so no retroactive-repair concern. Recommend a lint/validation rule flagging `human.correct` usage with a hint toward `human.approval` be considered as follow-up product work (out of this integration pass's scope to add unilaterally — a UX/validation-copy decision, not a mechanical fix).

Tests: `packages/blocks-core/src/human/correct.test.ts` (rewritten), `packages/blocks-core/src/catalog.test.ts` (comment only, assertions unchanged and still passing). Full suite green post-fix.

## 2026-07-11 — S9 Phase 3 (reconciliation ledger item 14 — CI/tooling)

### A29 — Redaction-bypass lint: a genuine cross-file precision gap found on its first real repo-wide run, partially closed; honest remaining count disclosed, not hidden

`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint.ts`/`redaction-lint-cli.ts` existed with a partial precision fix already landed earlier this integration pass (the `isGuardedByInjectedRedactFn` argument-passing regex broadening). It had never actually been RUN across the full merged repo until this reconciliation item — running it for the first time (`pnpm run lint:redaction`, newly wired as a root script + CI step, see below) produced **205 findings**, an order of magnitude more than plausible for a codebase this deliberate about redaction discipline.

**Root cause of the majority (205 → 187 after this fix):** `findRedactFnBoundIdentifiers` scans ONLY the current file's own source text for a `<name>: RedactFn` type declaration to recognize the constructor-injection pattern. `@aart/engine`'s real structure declares `redact: RedactFn` exactly ONCE (`EngineConfig`, `packages/engine/src/types.ts`) — every OTHER engine file that actually PERSISTS data (`run-lifecycle.ts`, `step-executor.ts`, `wait/wait-machine.ts`, `concurrency.ts`) only ever sees `config.redact` (a property access with no local type annotation in that file's own text), so the per-file scan found ZERO bound identifiers in every one of them and could never recognize the guard. Confirmed directly, not assumed: `run-lifecycle.ts:95`'s flagged `await config.store.runs.put(redacted)` sits ONE LINE after `const redacted = applyRedaction(config.redact, run, resolvedSecretRefs)` in the exact same function — a textbook false positive from this exact cause, not a real bypass.

**Fix:** `lintRedactionBypass` now does two passes — reads every file in the scan batch once, unions each file's own `RedactFn`-bound identifiers into ONE repo-wide set, then lints every file against that combined set (`lintSource` gained an optional `extraBoundIdentifiers` parameter, additive and backward-compatible — every existing test, which deliberately keeps a fixture's declaration and usage in the same string, is unaffected). This closed every finding in `run-lifecycle.ts` and materially reduced `step-executor.ts`/`concurrency.ts`/`wait-machine.ts` (205 → 187 total).

**Honest disclosure — NOT triaged to zero within this session's time budget.** 187 findings remain, breaking down as: 147 in `@aart/mcp` (the large majority — a first pass suggests most are the `mcp/stubs/**` fake-implementation surface, which by construction never handles real secrets, plus some genuine MCP-tool-return call sites worth a closer look), 14 `@aart/server`, 7 `@aart/familiarity-evals` (this session's own new `ci-gate.ts` — plain progress/status `console.log` calls, e.g. `console.log(`  [${status}] ${r.taskId}...`)`, not an application-record echo), 7 `@aart/dashboard`, 4 `@aart/engine`, 2 each `@aart/registry`/`@aart/llm`/`@aart/evidence`/`@aart/cli`, 1 `@aart/governance`. At least 67 of the 187 sit in files literally named `stub*`/under a `stubs/` directory — deterministic fake-data implementations, structurally incapable of ever handling a real secret, the same "operates below where redaction applies" reasoning the CLI's own `DEFAULT_EXCLUDED_PACKAGES = new Set(["store"])` already establishes for `@aart/store`'s adapter/conformance layer — a strong candidate for a similar, explicit exclusion, not attempted here for lack of time to verify EVERY stub file individually rather than pattern-match by filename. The remainder needs one-by-one triage (real bypass vs. a heuristic gap of the same general shape as the one just fixed, e.g. a guard call in the CALLER's stack frame rather than the persisting function's own block) that this session's time budget did not allow finishing. **Recommend a follow-up session complete this triage to a genuine zero** (or an explicitly-justified, reviewed suppression list) before treating this gate as a hard CI blocker — it is wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` as an informational/non-blocking step for now (see that file), not yet a merge-blocking gate, specifically because it is not yet at a trustworthy zero.

### A30 — Familiarity-evals: real `ValidateFn`/`RunSuccessFn` wiring + fake-model CI gate (this package's own DoD: "S9 wires the real engine-backed check in later")

`packages/familiarity-evals`'s own `validate.ts`/`run-success.ts`/`index.ts` doc comments explicitly named this session's job: replace the package's lightweight REFERENCE validator/run-success stand-ins with real `@aart/governance`/`@aart/engine`-backed checks, and wire the already-built fake-model adapter (`model-runner.ts`'s `createFakeModelRunner`, built by S6) into an actual CI entry point — neither had been done. New `packages/familiarity-evals/src/real-checks.ts` (`createRealValidateFn`/`createRealRunSuccessFn`, mirroring `packages/mcp/src/real-context.ts`'s/`packages/dashboard/src/capability-catalog.ts`'s established real-composition pattern, not imported from either — each consumer calls the same underlying `@aart/governance`/`@aart/blocks-core` functions, none depends on a sibling's own composition root) and `ci-gate.ts` (a real CI entry point, scripted responses for all 8 `AUTHORING_TASK_CATALOG` tasks, wired as `pnpm run eval:familiarity`).

Two real bugs found and fixed via actually running this against the real engine (not assumed working from a clean typecheck): (1) `createRealRunSuccessFn` forgot `store.workflows.put(workflow)` before `triggerRun` — `triggerRun`'s own documented contract doesn't persist the Workflow itself (S1 SEAMS Seam 3), a precondition every other real-engine call site in this repo already follows; fixed. (2) `llm.call`'s real dispatch needs a provider key `call-llm-with-schema`'s task doesn't have (none available in this environment) — fixed by injecting the same fake-Anthropic-client pattern (this session's own `RealCatalogLlmOptions` addition to `real-context.ts`) into `real-checks.ts`'s own catalog construction, always-on (a run-success check should never depend on a real external provider call regardless of whether a key happens to be configured — a hermeticity/determinism concern, not just this environment's missing key).

**Honest disclosure:** spec §32.4 wants familiarity-eval baselines run "against real target models, including a genuinely weak one." This gate runs the fake model only — no LLM provider API key is available in this environment (verified: none of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`/`GOOGLE_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY` are set). It proves the HARNESS (real validation, real run-success execution, deterministic scoring) is wired correctly and stays a genuine regression gate — but it does not satisfy §32.4's full intent of measuring a real model's zero-shot behavior. A real-model baseline run is necessary, documented follow-up once a provider key is available. Same disclosure shape as the plan's own pre-existing Risk 6 (the §9.3 release-verification E2E's fixture-only fallback) — flagged explicitly rather than letting a weaker validation silently read as equivalent-strength evidence.

### A31 — Strict test-file typechecking added; real, previously-hidden type errors found and fixed

`tsconfig.base.json`'s `exclude: ["**/*.test.ts", ...]` means `tsc -b` (both `pnpm run build` and `pnpm run typecheck`) has NEVER type-checked a single `*.test.ts` file across this entire repo — only vitest's own (non-type-checking, esbuild-based) transform ever touched them. New root `tsconfig.test-typecheck.json` (flat `include` of every package's `src/**/*.ts`, `composite`/`declaration`/emit all off — a pure `--noEmit` pass, not a build) + `pnpm run typecheck:tests`, wired as its own CI step.

Running it for the first time surfaced real, previously-invisible type errors, all fixed: `packages/engine/src/step-executor.test.ts` imported `AartStore` from `@aart/types` (it lives in `@aart/store`) — a genuine wrong import that happened to work under vitest's looser transform. Six `wait`/`human` block test files (`for-external-job`, `for-queue`, `for-signal`, `for-webhook`, `manual`, `until`, `human/approval`) spread a block's `execute()` return value (typed `Promise<unknown>` by `BlockImplementation`'s own frozen contract — intentional, blocks may return anything) directly into an object literal (`{...result, schemaVersion: 1}`) — a real `TS2698` spreading `unknown`; fixed with a minimal `as Record<string, unknown>` cast per site (the block contract itself is correctly unchanged). `packages/blocks-core/src/browser/goto.test.ts` and this session's own `human/correct.test.ts` accessed a property directly on an `unknown`-typed result; fixed to use `toMatchObject`/a narrow cast, matching each file's own sibling-test style. `packages/llm/src/blocks/judge.test.ts` constructed an object literal with `recordLlmCall` against a plain `BlockExecutionContext`-typed parameter (excess-property check) — the real target type, `EngineBlockExecutionContext` (added to `@aart/engine` earlier this integration pass for reconciliation item 6), was never actually re-exported from `@aart/engine`'s own public `index.ts` — a genuine gap in that earlier work, found only because this new check exists; fixed by exporting it and giving `@aart/llm` a `devDependency` on `@aart/engine` (test-only) plus the matching `tsconfig.json` project reference.

### A32 — S5's CLI `bin.js` verified working from the merged root; `.skip`/`.only` and CJS/ESM ajv-interop cross-checks: clean, no action needed

- `bin.js` (`packages/cli`): verified it runs correctly against the full merged monorepo (not just S5's own isolated Wave-1 worktree) — see this session's final report for the exact invocation and output.
- Grepped every package's `src/**/*.test.ts` for `.skip(`/`.only(`/`describe.skip`/`it.only` etc.: zero matches repo-wide.
- Grepped for `ajv`-style CJS default-export-interop usage: exactly one real usage (`packages/llm/src/validate-output.ts`, `import { Ajv, type ErrorObject } from "ajv"` — a NAMED import against a CJS package with no `exports` map). Verified genuinely correct at REAL runtime, not just under vitest's transform: directly `node`-imported the compiled `packages/llm/dist/validate-output.js` and executed `validateAgainstSchema` against a real schema — resolved and ran without error. No bug found.
- Re-verified `Workflow.concurrency` (S1's A16 amendment) is visible everywhere it needs to be: `packages/governance/src/approval-summary.ts`'s `COVERED_WORKFLOW_FIELDS` already includes it (fixed at merge 3, per this file's earlier entries) and that file's own `trust-surface-completeness.test.ts` — an ADR-17 CI-enforced gate — already passes, confirming the coverage is structurally exhaustive, not just textually present. No other file in the repo references `.concurrency` outside `packages/engine/src/concurrency.ts` (the mechanism itself) and this one governance file — nothing else needed updating.

### CI wiring (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`)

Added, after the existing `Typecheck` step and before `Test`: `Typecheck — test files` (`pnpm run typecheck:tests`, A31), `Lint — redaction bypass scan` (`pnpm run lint:redaction`, A29 — `continue-on-error: true`, informational only, per A29's own honest-disclosure note above: not yet at a trustworthy zero), `Familiarity-evals CI gate` (`pnpm run eval:familiarity`, A30, after `Test` since it needs each package's own `dist/` build output, same as `lint:redaction`).

## 2026-07-11 — S9 Phase 3 (publish-readiness) — CRITICAL FINDING, not one of the pre-enumerated reconciliation ledger items

### A33 — `@team-monet/aart` (the CLI) is currently UNINSTALLABLE by any real end user — depends on 3 packages marked `private: true` that can never be published, and no bundling step exists to avoid that

**Severity: the single highest-priority publish-readiness finding.** Everything else in publish-readiness (version stamping, README, changelog) is cosmetic if the package itself doesn't install. It doesn't, as currently structured.

**Reproduction (concrete, not inferred):** `pnpm pack` in `packages/cli` correctly resolves `workspace:*` protocol specifiers to real version numbers (`"@aart/mcp": "0.1.0"`, etc. — verified directly by inspecting the packed tarball's `package.json`; a first attempt with plain `npm pack` left the literal, invalid `"workspace:*"` string in place, which would be an even more immediately obvious break — using the correct tool, `pnpm pack`/`pnpm publish --dry-run`, still does not fix the underlying problem). Installing the resulting tarball in a genuinely isolated directory (`npm init -y && npm install ./team-monet-aart-0.1.0.tgz`, no access to this workspace's own `node_modules`, simulating a real end user) fails immediately:

```
npm error code E404
npm error 404 Not Found - GET https://registry.npmjs.org/@aart%2fmcp - Not found
npm error 404 '@aart/mcp@0.1.0' is not in this registry.
```

**Root cause:** `packages/cli/package.json`'s real `dependencies` are `@aart/mcp`, `@aart/store`, `@aart/types` — all three marked `"private": true` in their own `package.json` (confirmed directly). A private package cannot be published under any name; none of the three exists on the real npm registry and none ever will as currently configured. `packages/cli`'s own build (`"build": "tsc -b"`) is plain TypeScript compilation — it transpiles, it does not bundle; every `import` of `@aart/mcp`/`@aart/store`/`@aart/types` in the compiled `dist/` output remains a real, unresolved-at-publish-time module specifier.

**This is not a new architecture question — it's a documented requirement that was never implemented, discovered via a spec-internal inconsistency worth flagging in its own right.** Architecture ADR-18 explicitly requires exactly this to work: "`packages/cli`... **bundles the runtime it depends on** per the existing bundle design (§0.3, ADR-04): a consumer running `npx @team-monet/aart` or `npm install @team-monet/aart` gets **one package** whose own dependency graph pulls in the `@aart/*` workspace packages it needs **at publish/build time**, not 14 separately-installed packages." That requirement is correct and exactly matches what this finding proves is missing. But ADR-18's own cross-reference for *how* — "the existing bundle design (§0.3, ADR-04)" — points at the WRONG mechanism: §0.3/ADR-04's "BUNDLE" is `aart bundle`'s workflow-deployment artifact (`manifest.json` + workflow/block/pack definitions + registry entries + trigger config, for Docker/K8s/serverless deployment of a *workflow*) — a real, already-implemented, differently-purposed thing (`packages/server/src/bundle/**`), structurally unrelated to bundling this CLI package's own *npm module dependency graph* for publication. Citing it as the mechanism for the latter is a genuine spec-internal inconsistency (the same class of gap this whole document already tracks via lettered amendments) — ADR-18 states a correct requirement and points at an inapplicable existing design to satisfy it.

**Not fixed here — flagged for a deliberate decision, not a unilateral one.** Actually resolving this means choosing and implementing a real JS dependency-bundling strategy for `packages/cli`'s build (most likely candidate: a bundler such as esbuild added to `packages/cli`'s own build step, configured to inline the pure-JS `@aart/*` workspace closure while marking genuine native-addon third-party dependencies — `isolated-vm` in particular, a compiled V8-isolate addon reachable transitively through `@aart/blocks-core`'s node-sandbox — as external, so npm's own install-time native build step still runs on the end consumer's machine rather than being broken by inlining). This is real, non-trivial implementation and verification work (the bundled CLI's ~20 commands all need to still function correctly, including the ones this session's own E2E/CLI-verification work already exercised for real — `init`/`validate`/`list`/`worker`/`server`/`mcp`) and a genuine architecture-level call (bundler choice, external-dependency boundary, whether `playwright` needs the same treatment) squarely outside a mechanical fix — not attempted here. **This blocks any real `npm publish`/`pnpm publish` of `@team-monet/aart` until resolved**, regardless of version number or README quality.

Verification: reproduced via `pnpm pack` + isolated `npm install` in a scratch directory (not the workspace) — real, concrete failure, not inferred from reading code. No source files changed by this finding; `AMENDMENTS.md` only.
