## 2026-07-13 — V2 Wave 2A: the activity feed, live

### A66 — V2 Wave 2A: the activity-feed dashboard view + live updates — `ApiClient.listEvents`, `GET /api/events` (backfill) + `GET /api/events/stream` (SSE broadcast), and `ActivityFeedPage.tsx`; parity id-routes for approvals/corrections/evals/deployments deliberately deferred, one with a genuine blocker found and flagged

John-ratified 2026-07-12 alongside the D2+V1 wave, "Wave 2 please" 2026-07-13. Branch `v2/activity-feed`, forked from `main`@`4100873` (the wave1/integration merge — D2b remote reads + V1 event log foundation, AMENDMENTS.md A61-A63). Product framing: "agent chat on the left, dashboard on the right, following live" — the visible payoff of the wave's VISIBILITY SYMMETRY intent. File-ownership boundary held throughout (two concurrent Wave-2 sibling sessions run in parallel on `aart watch`/packages/cli and approve-against-remote/packages/mcp): every file this session touched is under `packages/dashboard/**`, plus one narrow, flagged exception in `packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` (below) and this file.

**Foundation consumed, not built here:** V1's event log (AMENDMENTS.md A61, fix-passed by A63) already appends every lifecycle transition to the 17th `AartStore` member and serves it at the real server's `GET /events?since=&limit=` (open/unauthenticated — A63 FIX 2 — `{ events: EventLogEntry[] }`, newest-first). This session makes that log VISIBLE and LIVE in the dashboard; it adds no new event types and touches no write site.

**PART 1 — `ApiClient.listEvents(since?, limit?)` (`packages/dashboard/src/api-client.ts`).** Mirrors `listRuns`'s dual-implementation shape exactly. `createHttpApiClient`: GETs the real server's `/events`, attaching `deployAuthHeaders` on the same terms as `listRuns`/`getRun`/`listFlaggedRunsViaApi` even though `GET /events` stays open regardless of `deployToken` configuration (A63 FIX 2) — harmless, and keeps this client uniform against a future session that gates `/events` too. `createFakeApiClient`: `store.events.list({since, limit})`, a plain passthrough — deliberately NOT re-deriving `@aart/server`'s own `parseEventsLimit` default/clamp policy locally, since that policy exists to defend an unauthenticated HTTP-exposed route, not a same-process local store read (the same reasoning every other `list*` method on this fake client already follows — none of them impose an artificial cap either). Tests: fake-client store round-trip (ordering, since-is-inclusive, limit) plus two real-`@aart/server` tests (read-through, and — the one genuinely worth asserting explicitly — succeeding with NO `deployToken` configured against a token-gated server, proving this route really does stay open).

**PART 2 — dashboard routes (`packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`).** `GET /api/events` → `api.listEvents(since, limit)` → a bare `EventLogEntry[]` (matching every sibling list route's response shape in this file — `/api/environments`, `/api/deployments`, `/api/approvals`, `/api/corrections` — none wrap in an envelope object; the real server's own `{ events }` shape is unwrapped one layer down, inside `listEvents` itself). `GET /api/events/stream`: a read-only Server-Sent-Events broadcast of events NEW since the connection opened — writes SSE headers + `flushHeaders()` + an initial `:ok\n\n` comment, then a `setInterval` (default 1500ms, new `DashboardConfig.eventsStreamPollIntervalMs`, `config.ts` — overridable mainly so tests don't need multi-second real-time waits) polls `api.listEvents(cursor)` and writes each newly-observed event as `data: ${JSON.stringify(event)}\n\n`, oldest-of-the-batch first (so sequential client-side prepends land newest-on-top); `req.on("close")` clears the interval and ends the response. Never touches the real server's own ticker — purely additive reads through the existing `ApiClient` boundary, same single-instance-constraint respected as everything else in this file.

**The cursor mechanism, and a genuine self-review course-correction worth recording in full (not just the tidy final answer):** `EventLogStore.list`'s own `since` filter is INCLUSIVE (`occurredAt >= since`, both adapters), so a plain "occurredAt > cursor" comparison would silently drop same-millisecond ties (e.g. `aart_approve`'s own 3-event emission, A63 FIX 4) — `sentIdsAtCursor` tracks ids already sent at the exact cursor timestamp to handle that. The FIRST shipped design derived the starting cursor from a one-off async `listEvents(undefined, 1)` "seed" read before the first recurring tick, deliberately NOT marking that seed's own batch as already-sent (so a concurrent append racing the seed's own read wouldn't be silently swallowed). That design was committed, then questioned during self-review: is "seed doesn't mark-as-sent" actually sufficient under a real concurrent-append race, or just usually-fine? Investigated directly rather than assumed either way — reintroduced a fully-marking seed and ran the concurrent-append test (passed, but only because the store was still empty when that particular run's seed read fired — inconclusive); widened the race window with a temporary 80ms artificial delay inside the seed path to force the test's append to reliably land inside the seed's own in-flight read, then reran against the ACTUAL shipped design — still passed, confirming the asymmetric guard was already correct (the first recurring tick's own inclusive `since: cursor` query re-observes anything at that exact timestamp, and since `sentIdsAtCursor` was never populated by the seed, it gets broadcast). Not a bug fix, then — but simplified anyway: that correctness depended on a non-obvious two-kinds-of-ticks asymmetry a future reader would have to re-derive by the same case analysis. Replaced with one mechanism — `cursor` is anchored to `clock.nowIso()` captured SYNCHRONOUSLY right after the connection opens, no `await` between opening the stream and setting it, so no async read and therefore no race window exists AT ALL, provably by construction rather than by case analysis. One unified `tick()`, no `seed` parameter. All 115 backend tests green against the final version, including the exact concurrent-append scenario both designs were checked against (`server.test.ts`, "GET /api/events/stream opens... and broadcasts a newly-appended event... through the real composition path").

No redaction wrapper on either route — `EventLogEntry` carries only run-lifecycle metadata (id/type/occurredAt/summary + correlation ids), never trace/inputs/outputs (no such field exists on the schema at all), the identical reasoning A63 FIX 2 already applied to the real server's own `GET /events`. One redaction-lint suppression added (`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` — a narrow, flagged exception to this session's own dashboard-only file-ownership boundary, purely additive, same precedent A62's own Deviation 1 set for an equivalent single-line necessary reach outside its nominal scope) for the poll tick's own `console.error`: individually justified — `err` is a read failure, never event/record data, and `EventLogEntry` has no secret-bearing field for this call site to have bypassed redaction on regardless.

Tests against a REAL `@aart/server` instance + real `startDashboard(createHttpApiClient(...))` over real HTTP (the standing imperative, and this file's own established "prove the router wiring, not a mock" bar): `GET /api/events` backfill; `GET /api/events/stream` opens and broadcasts a newly-appended event within a couple 30ms poll intervals (interval overridden for test speed only, production default unchanged at 1500ms); disconnect cleanup (destroying the client connection never crashes the dashboard, proven behaviorally — the process keeps serving fresh requests immediately after, past several poll intervals' worth of real time).

**PART 3 — `ActivityFeedPage.tsx` (`packages/dashboard/frontend/src/pages/`).** Backfills once on mount (`GET /api/events?limit=50`), then opens a same-origin `EventSource('/api/events/stream')` and prepends incoming events, deduping by id (a just-backfilled event and the stream's own first delivery can legitimately overlap — both routes independently read the same log). Each row: an icon+color by event-type FAMILY (green = `run.completed`/`workflow.approved`; red = `run.failed`/`workflow.gate_failed`; blue = `approval.*`/`workflow.gate_passed`/`validated`; gray = everything else), a relative timestamp, and a link built from `runId` first, `workflowId` as fallback (a workflow-version-level gate/approval event with no run yet — `event-log.ts`'s own documented case), unlinked otherwise. `classifyEvent`'s fallback (unrecognized type → gray) matters for real: `EventLogEntry.type` is deliberately not a closed enum (`event-log.ts`'s own header comment), so a future write site's new type string must still render sensibly, not break.

`router.tsx`: new `activity` `parseRoute` case, mirroring `waiting-runs`/`flagged-runs`/`approvals`'s own flat shape. `App.tsx`: new nav entry (`Rss` icon — `Activity` is already this sidebar's own Evaluation Suites entry), placed FIRST in the nav list — **a judgment call, not explicitly specified by the brief**, made given this slice's own repeated framing as the wave's visible payoff; trivially reorderable if wrong, flagged here rather than silently decided. New `MainContent` switch case.

Tests (`ActivityFeedPage.test.tsx`): backfill render (newest-first as returned, plus an explicit assertion that the constructed `EventSource` URL is the bare same-origin-relative `/api/events/stream` — this package's own "the SPA never knows the real server's address" invariant, header comment, applies to the stream exactly as much as to every `fetch()` call) and live-prepend-with-dedupe (redelivering the same id twice yields exactly one row — proven directly, not just implied by the implementation). jsdom has no `EventSource` implementation (verified directly — neither jsdom nor plain Node 22 expose a global one) — a minimal `MockEventSource` stand-in, stubbed via `vi.stubGlobal` the same way `test-support/mock-fetch.ts` already stands in for `global.fetch`.

Manually verified end-to-end in a real browser (a running dashboard, `createFakeApiClient` over a seeded fs store, one example of every documented event type plus a live append 4s after boot): correct colors/icons per family, correct relative timestamps, correct links — spot-checked via the rendered DOM's actual `href` attributes (`document.querySelectorAll('a')`), not just visual inspection, after an initial visual misread of which row a click landed on turned out to be tester error, not a code bug — the live event appeared at the top with the "Live" indicator lit, with no page reload.

**PARITY ID-ROUTES (V1 Slice 6) — deliberately deferred, not silently dropped.** The brief's own explicit fallback ("ship the feed even if you run low… note which parity routes you deferred") was exercised. Evaluated each of the four before deciding, not deferred by default:

- **Approvals — a genuine blocker found, not just deprioritized.** `GET /api/approvals` (`server.ts`) hardcodes `api.listApprovals("pending")` — it can never return an already-decided task. An `approval.decided` activity-feed event, by definition, points at a task that is no longer pending — building `/approvals/:id` today would mean shipping a link that reliably 404s for exactly the events it exists to serve. Flagged as a separate task (`task_fd445f45`, spawned this session) rather than fixed inline: fixing it means deciding between an optional `?status=` query param (preserving `ApprovalsPage.tsx`'s current pending-only default) and/or a new single-entity `GET /api/approvals/:id` + `ApiClient.getApproval`, a real design choice outside this slice's own scope.
- **Corrections/evals/deployments — not blocked (their list routes already return everything unconditionally, no status gate), but deferred anyway on time/risk budget.** Even at the brief's own "minimum" bar (route to the existing list page, highlight/scroll to the matching row — no new backend route needed), this touches three more existing, already-tested page files (`CorrectionsPage.tsx`, `EvalsPage.tsx`, `ProductionPage.tsx`) each needing its own re-verification, plus router/App wiring, plus widening `ActivityFeedPage.tsx`'s own `linkForEvent`. Judged better done as its own focused pass than squeezed into this session's tail end alongside the two-clean-full-gate-run requirement — the core feed (this slice's own explicit priority) is what got the full attention instead.

`linkForEvent` (`ActivityFeedPage.tsx`) links `runId`-then-`workflowId` only, today — the two routes that genuinely, robustly resolve; correlation-id-only events (deployment/eval/correction-only) render unlinked rather than pointing at a route that doesn't exist.

**Deviations flagged, not silent:**
1. Nav entry position (App.tsx, Activity Feed placed first) — a judgment call, see PART 3 above.
2. `packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` touched — a narrow, additive, individually-justified exception to this session's own dashboard-only file-ownership boundary (PART 2 above), required to keep `lint:redaction` green; no other file in that package touched.
3. `DashboardConfig.eventsStreamPollIntervalMs` is new public surface on a package whose own doc comments elsewhere (`config.ts`) mirror `@aart/server`'s "documented tunables with defaults" convention — added for the identical reason `clock`/`workerUrls` are already injectable there (test determinism), not exposed as a CLI/env-var knob anywhere; production behavior is unchanged (default 1500ms, exactly the ratified design's own specified interval).
4. `createFakeApiClient.listEvents` (the local/embedded `aart dev` topology) has no artificial page-size cap, while the real HTTP route defaults to 100 and hard-clamps at 1000 (`@aart/server`'s `parseEventsLimit`) — a deliberate, not accidental, asymmetry: the cap is a defense for an unauthenticated network-exposed route, not a same-process direct store read, and matches this fake client's own existing behavior for every other `list*` method (none of which impose a cap either). `ActivityFeedPage.tsx`'s own backfill fetch always sends an explicit `?limit=50` regardless, so this asymmetry has no practical effect on the shipped page.
5. One stale, already-resolved flagged task from a prior session (`task_75e1f39f`, "Add GET /events to DEPLOY.md's open-routes table") was found and dismissed in passing, with direct evidence (`DEPLOY.md:386` already carries A63 FIX 2's note) — not this session's own finding to claim credit for, noted here only because dismissing another session's flagged item deserves a paper trail.

**Tests, summarized:** backend +14 (`api-client.test.ts` +3: fake-client `listEvents`, real-server `listEvents` read-through, `listEvents` staying open with no token against a token-gated server; `server.test.ts` +4: `GET /api/events` fake-backed, plus a new real-server describe block with 3 cases — backfill, stream-opens-and-broadcasts, disconnect-cleanup); frontend +2 (`ActivityFeedPage.test.tsx`: backfill render, live-prepend-with-dedupe). `makeEvent()` fixtures added on both sides (`test-support/fixtures.ts`, backend and frontend), mirroring every existing per-entity factory.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && pnpm run check:tsconfig-refs && pnpm run build && pnpm run typecheck && pnpm run typecheck:tests && pnpm run lint:redaction && pnpm test`): **237 files / 2561 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (A63's own 236/2552 baseline + this session's own net +1 file / +9 tests). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs — **262 reviewed suppressions** (A63's own 261 baseline + this session's own single new entry, PART 2 above). Both full run logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run. This session's own new tests involve a real `setInterval`-driven poll and a real raw streaming HTTP connection (genuine timer-based execution, not just ordinary async/await) — judged closer to `migration-race.e2e.test.ts`'s own flakiness-risk class than an ordinary request/response test, so the new `GET /api/events`/`GET /api/events/stream` describe block was run 5 additional times in isolation beyond the two full-suite passes (7 total observed passes) — clean every time, zero flakiness.

## 2026-07-13 — Wave 2 fix pass

### A67 — Wave 2 fix pass: SSE stream burst-drop closed, `aart watch` shutdown SIGKILL dead-code closed, dashboard frontend now ships inside the published CLI (John's ratified FIX 3 choice), plus re-entrancy/readiness-timeout/feed-cap/comment minors

John ratified the D2+V1 wave 2026-07-12, Wave 2 2026-07-13, and chose "ship the dashboard frontend in the CLI package" for FIX 3 below. This session addresses the findings from Wave 2's own three-perspective verification (cold auditor + briefed reviewer + real-binary e2e) against the three Wave 2 sibling sessions merged into `wave2/integration` (A64 `aart watch`, A65 remote-approve, A66 the activity feed) — worked in the pre-existing isolated worktree/branch `wave2/integration` (tip `3f718f2` at start), committed incrementally, nothing pushed.

**The meta-point driving this pass, stated up front because it shaped how every fix below was verified:** the two worst bugs here (watch shutdown, dashboard-frontend-not-found) were INVISIBLE to the pre-existing 2624-test unit suite — they only manifest through real esbuild bundling, a real `npm install -g` symlink, and real OS process/signal timing. So (a) the unit tests fixed/added below model REAL Node semantics rather than a fixture's own assumptions about them, and (b) FIX 3 was additionally verified against a genuinely packed-and-globally-installed tarball, not just unit-level.

#### FIX 1 — MAJOR: SSE live stream drops events under burst (cold-auditor finding), closed

`packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`'s `GET /api/events/stream` tick read only the newest `DEFAULT_EVENTS_LIMIT` (100) events since `cursor` (`EventLogStore.list`'s own contract — newest-first, `LIMIT` applied after `ORDER BY occurred_at DESC`), then unconditionally advanced `cursor = batch[0].occurredAt` (the newest). A burst of more than 100 events landing inside one ~1.5s poll window silently, PERMANENTLY excluded everything strictly between "newest" and "the 100th newest" — every later tick's own `occurredAt >= cursor` filter skipped right over them, forever.

Fixed with a new `drainNewEventsSince(api, cursor, initialLimit)` helper (`server.ts:214`, called from `tick()` at `server.ts:578`) that re-queries `since: cursor` (unchanged) with a GROWING limit (`server.ts:170`, `MAX_DRAIN_ATTEMPTS = 6`: 100→200→400→800→1600→3200) until either a partial page proves nothing was truncated, or growth plateaus (a real HTTP-backed `ApiClient`'s own `GET /events` hard-clamps at `@aart/server`'s `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT`, 1000 — asking for more than that can never return more). **Known, accepted residual, not silently left unstated:** `EventLogStore.list` has only `since`+`limit`, no upper-bound/continuation-token parameter at all, so a burst of genuinely more than 1000 events landing strictly between two ticks still can't be fully recovered in ONE tick via this API shape alone. **CORRECTED below (re-review correction addendum) — this paragraph originally claimed the cursor "keeps draining across subsequent ticks rather than losing anything"; that is false.** The cursor still advances straight to the batch's own newest event's timestamp regardless of the cap, so the overflow beyond 1000 is a permanent live-stream drop, not a self-healing one — see the addendum at the end of this section for the full corrected explanation, the dead-warning bug the same re-review found alongside it, and both fixes. Closing the underlying residual completely needs a real store/HTTP pagination-shape change, outside this fix pass's own scope. Below the 1000 ceiling — the actual scenario the task described ("if >100 events land in one poll window") — drainage is fully correct.

**Test, and the pre-fix-fail verification the task required:** `server.test.ts` — "delivers ALL events from a burst larger than DEFAULT_EVENTS_LIMIT — none silently dropped" (150 events, `DEFAULT_EVENTS_LIMIT + 50`) seeds the ENTIRE burst, with `occurredAt` timestamped in the future relative to real "now," BEFORE the stream ever opens — deliberately, not seeded-after-open the way the sibling "evt-live" test does. An earlier draft seeded after-open and passed even against the pre-fix code, for a real reason worth recording: the 30ms poll interval interleaved with the still-in-progress `for`-loop seeding and drained it incrementally, a few events per tick, never actually landing >100 in any ONE tick — the exact trap a "burst" test can fall into without noticing. Rewritten to seed everything up front; **verified directly against the pre-fix single-page tick (temporarily reverting only the `tick()` body's `drainNewEventsSince` call back to a bare `api.listEvents(cursor, DEFAULT_EVENTS_LIMIT)`, fixture unchanged): the test times out at the 5000ms suite default (the dropped middle ~50 never arrive) — confirmed FAILING pre-fix, then reverted and confirmed PASSING post-fix.**

#### FIX 2 — BLOCKER: `aart watch` shutdown SIGKILL is dead code → real orphaning (real-binary-tester + reviewer findings), closed

`packages/cli/src/commands/watch.ts`'s `shutdownChildren` trusted `child.killed` as a proxy for "has this child exited" — real Node's `killed` reflects "a signal was SENT" (set synchronously inside `.kill()` itself), never "the process has died." Two bugs, same root cause:

1. **The SIGKILL escalation was dead code.** `child.kill(signal)` ran, and the SAME `graceMs` timer's own callback then checked `if (!child.killed) child.kill("SIGKILL")` — but `killed` was already `true` by the time that timer could ever fire, for every child, always. A slow-draining child (the dashboard — `serve-dashboard.mjs`'s `stop()` awaited `server.close()`, which doesn't resolve until every open connection, including an idle keep-alive one or one of the dashboard's own long-lived SSE streams, closes on its own) was silently left running past `graceMs`, orphaned, while `aart watch` printed `"Watch stopped."` (`ok: true`) as if shutdown had genuinely completed.
2. **The pre-filter didn't skip an already-self-exited (crashed) child.** `children.filter(c => !c.killed)` only skips a child someone already called `.kill()` ON — a child that exited entirely on its own has `killed === false` forever, so it wasn't skipped: `shutdownChildren` would re-signal an already-dead process and register a `once("exit")` listener that can never fire (that event already happened), stalling for the FULL `graceMs`.

Fixed by switching every check to a new `hasExited(child)` helper (`watch.ts:403`: `child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null`, which Node only ever sets on a REAL exit) — the exact verbatim diff:

```diff
 export interface ChildLike {
   readonly killed: boolean;
+  readonly exitCode: number | null;
+  readonly signalCode: NodeJS.Signals | null;
   kill(signal?: NodeJS.Signals): boolean;
   once(event: "exit", listener: (code: number | null, signal: NodeJS.Signals | null) => void): void;
 }
 ...
+function hasExited(child: ChildLike): boolean {
+  return child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null;
+}
 ...
 export async function shutdownChildren(children: readonly ChildLike[], options: ShutdownOptions): Promise<void> {
   const signal = options.signal ?? "SIGTERM";
-  const alive = children.filter((c) => !c.killed);
+  const alive = children.filter((c) => !hasExited(c));
   await Promise.all(
     alive.map(
       (child) =>
         new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
           const timer = setTimeout(() => {
-            if (!child.killed) child.kill("SIGKILL");
+            if (!hasExited(child)) child.kill("SIGKILL");
             resolve();
           }, options.graceMs);
           child.once("exit", () => {
             clearTimeout(timer);
             resolve();
           });
           child.kill(signal);
         }),
     ),
   );
 }
```

**MANDATORY fixture fix.** `watch.test.ts`'s own `createFakeChild()` set `killed = true` inside `emitExit()` — CONFLATING "exited on its own" with "killed by us," the same wrong mental model the implementation had, which is exactly why every pre-existing test in this file passed despite the bug (two wrongs agreeing with each other, hiding it from the whole suite — the identical class of failure root AMENDMENTS.md A27 already named for a different bug). Fixed to model real Node precisely: `.kill()` now sets `killed = true` synchronously (it didn't before) but never touches `exitCode`/`signalCode`; `emitExit()` now sets `exitCode`/`signalCode` (never `killed`).

**Tests, and the pre-fix-fail verification the task required** (`watch.test.ts`, `describe("shutdownChildren")`): (i) "a child that already self-exited (crashed) before shutdownChildren was even called resolves promptly, without stalling for graceMs or re-signaling it" — `graceMs: 10_000`, deliberately far past vitest's own 5000ms default test timeout, so a regression to the old stall fails the test by timing out, not just by a wrong assertion; (ii) "escalates to SIGKILL after graceMs when a child ignores SIGTERM and never exits" (the direct regression pin for bug 1); (iii) "sends SIGTERM and resolves without escalating once every child reports its own exit" (the normal case) — plus the pre-existing mixed-set and custom-signal tests, updated for the new fixture. **Verified directly against the pre-fix logic** (temporarily reverting only `shutdownChildren`'s body to `!child.killed`, fixture left at its NEW, fixed shape): the escalation test (ii) and the mixed-set test both FAIL (`["SIGTERM"]` received where `["SIGTERM", "SIGKILL"]` was expected), and the self-exit test (i) TIMES OUT at 5000ms (confirming the stall) — then reverted and confirmed all pass post-fix.

**SECONDARY fix (make SIGTERM alone prompt, so SIGKILL is a true last resort):** `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`'s `startDashboard(...).close` called plain `server.close(cb)`, which waits for every open connection (including the dashboard's own long-lived SSE streams) to close on its own. Added `server.closeAllConnections?.()` immediately after `server.close(cb)` (Node 18.2+, well under this workspace's `engines.node: ">=22"` floor) — forces every open connection closed so the `close()` callback fires promptly instead of waiting on a keep-alive/SSE-stream timeout. `serve-dashboard.mjs`'s own `stop()` needed no separate change — it already calls this same `close()`. Not separately unit-tested (deterministically triggering the underlying Node behavior this defends against is closer to an integration property than a unit-testable one) — instead verified live, end to end, as part of FIX 3's own real-binary check below (the dashboard child reported `"[dashboard] stopped."` and the whole `aart watch` tree exited cleanly on SIGINT well inside `graceMs`, with zero orphans).

#### FIX 3 — BLOCKER: dashboard 404s from a real install, closed (John's ratified choice: ship the frontend inside the CLI package)

Two coupled problems, both closed:

**(a) PACKAGING — the real root cause.** The published CLI tarball shipped only `packages/cli/dist/*`; `packages/dashboard` (and its built frontend) never shipped at all, so there were no frontend assets for ANY `getFrontendDir()` candidate to find, regardless of path-guessing logic. Fixed in `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs`: after building `serve-dashboard.mjs` (unchanged), it now also copies `packages/dashboard/dist/frontend` (the built SPA) to `packages/cli/dist/frontend` — a direct sibling of `serve-dashboard.mjs`, `bin.js`, and `index.js` — inside the one package this monorepo actually publishes. Fails loudly (not silently) if the frontend hasn't been built yet, matching this codebase's A47 discipline. `packages/cli/scripts/build-publish.mjs`'s own dead-per-file-output sweep would otherwise delete the copied frontend's `.js`/`.js.map` chunks (indistinguishable from tsc's own dead per-file output to that sweep) — added a `NEVER_SWEEP_DIRS` guard so this is safe regardless of build-script ordering; **verified directly, not assumed**, by running the sweep with the guard removed and confirming it DOES delete `packages/cli/dist/frontend/assets/index-*.js`, then confirming the guard prevents it.

**(b) RESOLUTION.** `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`'s `getFrontendDir()` was widely assumed to need a new bundled-layout candidate. **Evidence contradicted that assumption** — verified empirically (a small esbuild ESM bundle built from a nested source path, then run from a differently-nested output path, still reports its OWN real runtime path via `import.meta.url`) before touching any code: `getFrontendDir()`'s EXISTING first candidate, `path.join(__dirname, "frontend")`, already resolves correctly once (a) exists — `__dirname` inside the bundled `serve-dashboard.mjs` is `packages/cli/dist` (esbuild rewrites `import.meta.url` to the bundle's real location), so `path.join(__dirname, "frontend")` is exactly `packages/cli/dist/frontend`, the copy's own target. No candidate-list change made; the function's doc comment was updated to explain why, rather than adding a dead-duplicate array entry. **Flagged as a deviation from the literal brief, not silently substituted** — see Deviations below.

**`aart watch` — symlink-safety + the installed layout.** `resolveWatchPaths`/`checkWatchPreconditions`/`buildDashboardEnv` (`watch.ts:122/181/259`) reworked: `process.argv[1]` is realpath-resolved FIRST via a new `resolveRealCliEntryPath` (`watch.ts:151`, best-effort, falls back to the raw path on failure) before any sibling-path derivation — a real `npm install -g`'s bin-shim is a SYMLINK, and deriving `serve-dashboard.mjs`/`frontend/` off the symlink's own directory (e.g. `/usr/local/bin`) finds nothing there. `WatchPaths.frontendDir` replaces the old `dashboardDistDir` (a monorepo-only "two directories up to `packages/dashboard/dist`" guess, no equivalent in a real global install) with a direct sibling of the CLI entry, matching the bundled/installed layout unconditionally. `buildDashboardEnv` now also sets `AART_DASHBOARD_FRONTEND_DIR` on the dashboard child directly (belt-and-braces — `getFrontendDir()`'s own candidate already finds it, but the parent already knows the exact answer). New `resolveRealCliEntryPath` tests use REAL temp-dir symlinks (`fs.symlink`), not a mock — fast, deterministic, exercises real OS semantics the way a fake couldn't.

**DOCS.** `DEPLOY.md` Path B and `AUTHORING.md` parts (b)/(f): both install recipes were missing `pnpm run build:dashboard-launcher` — without it, a real `npm install -g` never got `serve-dashboard.mjs` OR its frontend, independent of the packaging fix above. Added, with the "why" inline. `DEPLOY.md`'s "dashboard-equivalent without Docker" paragraph implied the monorepo checkout was still needed on the target host — corrected (the installed package's own `dist/` now has everything, run directly via a realpath-resolved sibling of the installed `aart` bin), and now names `aart watch` as the local/dev alternative. `AUTHORING.md`'s "(g) Honest limits" bullet flatly claimed "There is no `aart dashboard` on the authoring machine" and framed the dashboard as SERVER-only, never over the local authoring store — now WRONG as of this fix (`aart watch` boots a real dashboard against the authoring machine's OWN local store, from the exact same global install) — rewritten, while preserving the still-true "no bare `aart dashboard` subcommand" fact and the still-true `aart_get_report`/`aart_remote_run` caveats.

```
$ curl -s -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code}\n" http://127.0.0.1:14000/

$ curl -s http://127.0.0.1:14000/api/health
{"status":"ok"}
$ curl -s http://127.0.0.1:14000/ | head -c 300        # real SPA HTML (<!doctype html>...<title>frontend</title>...), not a 404
$ curl -s -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code}\n" http://127.0.0.1:14000/activity   # SPA client route via fallback
HTTP 200
$ curl -s -o /dev/null -w "HTTP %{http_code}\n" http://127.0.0.1:14000/assets/index-edgqyKwx.js   # real static asset
HTTP 200

$ kill -INT <watch-pid>
[server]   {"ok": true, "message": "Server stopped.", "port": 18080}
[dashboard] [dashboard] stopped.
[worker]   {"ok": true, "message": "Worker stopped."}
[watch] stopped.
{"ok": true, "message": "Watch stopped.", "ports": {"server": 18080, "worker": 8787, "dashboard": 14000}}
                                                       # SIGTERM alone was prompt — SIGKILL never needed (FIX 2's SECONDARY closeAllConnections fix in action), well inside the 10s graceMs

$ pgrep -fl "npm-prefix/lib/node_modules/@team-monet/aart|serve-dashboard"
CLEAN — no orphaned processes found
$ lsof -iTCP:18080,8787,14000 -sTCP:LISTEN
CLEAN — all three ports released
```

**Result: tarball includes `dist/frontend`, a real global install's `aart watch` serves the dashboard (`GET /` and `GET /api/health` both 200 against the real SPA), and shutdown is clean — zero orphaned processes, all ports released.** Every sub-check the brief asked for was completed; nothing was skipped or partial.

#### FIX 4 — SSE tick re-entrancy guard, closed

A slow `tick()` (its own `drainNewEventsSince` call now potentially several sequential round-trips under FIX 1) could still be in flight when `setInterval`'s next firing started a second one, racing the shared `cursor`/`sentIdsAtCursor` mutable state. Added a `tickRunning` flag (`server.ts:566`, checked/set/cleared inside `tick()` itself, `server.ts:575`) — an overlapping tick is skipped entirely, not queued; the next tick after that picks up from wherever `cursor` was actually left. Test: wraps a real `ApiClient` with an 80ms artificial delay on `listEvents` against a 20ms poll interval and asserts `maxInFlight === 1` while `callCount > 1` (ticking genuinely happened more than once, not accidentally serialized by a fluke).

#### FIX 5 — Readiness poll has no per-request timeout, closed

`pollUntilReady` (`watch.ts:342`) had no bound on any individual `fetch` attempt — Node's `fetch` has none by default — so a health endpoint that accepted the TCP connection but never responded could hang that ONE request forever, past the overall 30s deadline (which is only ever checked BETWEEN attempts). Added `AbortSignal.timeout(requestTimeoutMs)` per request (`watch.ts:350`, new `requestTimeoutMs` field, defaulting to a new `WATCH_READY_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 3_000`, injectable for tests). Test injects a fetch that never resolves unless aborted and confirms the poll still resolves (doesn't hang the test) within a small injected `requestTimeoutMs`.

#### FIX 6 — Frontend feed array cap, closed (minor)

`ActivityFeedPage.tsx`'s live SSE prepend had no bound — a long-open tab's `events` array (and its O(n) dedupe check per incoming frame) grew without limit. Capped to the newest `MAX_FEED_EVENTS` (500, `ActivityFeedPage.tsx:32`) after every prepend (`.slice(0, MAX_FEED_EVENTS)`, `ActivityFeedPage.tsx:149`). Test seeds a backfill already AT the cap (500), delivers one more live event, and asserts the oldest entry was evicted while the new one and the rest are retained — verified failing (the evicted entry still present) against the code with `.slice()` removed, then restored.

#### FIX 7 — SSE response `.on("error")`, closed (minor)

Added a defensive `ctx.res.on("error", ...)` (`server.ts:631`) alongside the pre-existing `req.on("close")`, both routed through one new idempotent `cleanup()` (clears the interval, ends the response, guarded against double-firing) — a silent network partition or a write against an already-broken socket can no longer surface as an unhandled stream `error` event and crash the whole dashboard process. Not independently unit-tested: deterministically triggering a genuine low-level socket `error` (as opposed to the ordinary client-disconnect `close` path, already covered by the pre-existing disconnect-cleanup test) from a black-box HTTP test isn't reliable without hooking into the server's own internals, which this fix's own small, low-risk scope didn't justify — flagged here rather than claiming coverage that doesn't exist.

#### FIX 8 — TRIVIAL: stale doc comment, closed

`packages/mcp/src/handlers/remote-governance.ts`'s `resume` field comment claimed `ResumeResult` embeds a redacted `RunRecord`; verified directly (`packages/server/src/engine/boundary.ts:62`) it never does — `ResumeResult` is `{kind:"resumed"|"duplicate", runId}` / `{kind:"no_match"}` / `{kind:"ambiguous", matches}`, no `RunRecord` field at all. Comment corrected; no code change.

The 269 baseline reconciles EXACTLY, not just plausibly, once the three-way `wave2/integration` merge is worked through arithmetically: A65's own MEASURED (not A63's merely stated) common-ancestor count, 262, plus A65's own 6 additions, plus A66's own 1 addition, plus A64's own 0 (`aart watch`'s `packages/cli` touches no `RunRecord`/trace-adjacent code) = 262+6+1+0 = **269** — confirming the three branches merged additively with no suppression entries lost or duplicated, not an unexplained drift. Both full run logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run. #### Re-review correction (John ratified 2026-07-13) — FIX 1's own doc comment overstated self-healing above MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT; the drain-cap warning was dead code

A fix re-review of FIX 1 above found two accuracy problems in what this entry and `server.ts` itself claimed — **not a core-logic bug: `drainNewEventsSince`'s actual delivery behavior (drains up to `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` per tick, no duplicates, nothing lost below that ceiling) was already correct and is UNCHANGED by this addendum.** Both problems are in what was said ABOUT that behavior once a burst exceeds `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` (1000) in one tick. Worked directly on `wave2/integration` (tip `6c28b09` at start), committed incrementally, nothing pushed.

**Problem 1 — FALSE DOC CLAIM.** Both `drainNewEventsSince`'s own doc comment (`server.ts`, above the function) and FIX 1's paragraph above claimed that a burst bigger than 1000 events "keeps draining across subsequent ticks rather than losing anything" — i.e., self-healing. **This is false.** `tick()` (`server.ts:607`, cursor advanced at `server.ts:624-625`) always sets `cursor = batch[0].occurredAt` — the batch's own newest event, unaffected by the cap (`EventLogStore.list`'s contract sorts `ORDER BY occurred_at DESC` BEFORE `LIMIT` is applied, for both the fs and sqlite adapters — verified directly against both: `packages/store/src/adapters/fs/events.ts`'s `list()` sorts then `.slice(0, limit)`s; `packages/store/src/adapters/sqlite/stores/events.ts:101`'s query is `ORDER BY occurred_at DESC, id DESC` before `LIMIT` — so index 0 is always the true newest regardless of truncation). Once a single tick's own drain is capped at `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT`, the OLDER events that overflowed that tick's page are strictly older than the new cursor, so every LATER tick's own `occurredAt >= cursor` filter permanently skips them — a genuine, permanent LIVE-STREAM drop, not a self-heal. (The events are never lost from the SYSTEM — they stay in the event store and are still returned by `GET /api/events` backfill / a dashboard refresh; only the live SSE push skips them.) Proven directly: a burst of 1050 events in one tick delivers exactly 1000 live; the other 50 never arrive on the stream across 60+ subsequent ticks (no more events appended) — see the new test below.

**Problem 2 — DEAD WARNING.** The `console.warn` meant to surface exactly this condition (`server.ts`, end of `drainNewEventsSince`) compared the returned `batch.length` to the CALLER's own growing requested `limit` (`if (batch.length === limit)`), not to the SERVER's actual hard cap. Once that requested `limit` grows past `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` (the doubling sequence `100→200→400→800→1600`), a real HTTP-backed `ApiClient` always returns exactly `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` regardless of how much bigger `limit` gets — so `batch.length === limit` can never be true again, the loop's own final check reads the resulting partial-relative-to-`limit` page as "not truncated, done," and the warning is unreachable for any burst actually large enough to hit the cap. **Verified directly, pre-fix:** a test asserting the warning fires against a 1050-event burst fails with `expected "warn" to be called at least once` against the original comparison — confirmed BEFORE landing the fix below, by temporarily reverting only the two lines this fix touches (the early-return `return grown` and the `batch.length === limit` check) back to their pre-fix shape, rebuilding, and re-running the new test; then restoring the fix and confirming it passes.

**THE FIX, both problems:**

- **Docs corrected** (honest, specific — this codebase's standard is documented-not-hidden limits, not a false "nothing is lost" claim): `drainNewEventsSince`'s own doc comment (`server.ts:197-227`) rewritten to state the live-push-only, non-self-healing drop plainly, name the `ORDER BY ... DESC` mechanism that makes `batch[0]` always the true newest, and point at `GET /api/events`/a refresh as where the overflow is still reachable. FIX 1's own paragraph above corrected in place (the false "keeps draining... rather than losing anything" sentence replaced) and now points here for the full explanation.
- **The warning now actually fires** (`server.ts:252-271`): the check imports `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` from `@aart/server` (the same constant `GET /events`'s own `parseEventsLimit` clamps to, `packages/server/src/http/server.ts:328`) and compares `batch.length === MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` instead of `batch.length === limit` — the real "did this tick's drain hit the server's hard per-request ceiling" signal. Also closed the identical gap on the loop's OTHER exit path: the early-return branch (`if (grown.length === batch.length)`, taken when growing the limit further stops returning more) used to `return grown` directly, bypassing the warning check entirely — changed to `batch = grown; break;` so both exit paths share the one check (this codebase's real call site, `initialLimit = DEFAULT_EVENTS_LIMIT = 100` doubling by 2, never happens to land exactly on this early-return path AT the 1000 boundary today, but the fix closes the gap on principle rather than leaving a second copy of the same bug class one constant-tuning away from resurfacing). The new message names `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` and points at `GET /api/events`/a refresh as the recovery path, so an operator gets a real, actionable signal instead of a silent gap. The pre-existing redaction-lint suppression for this call site (`packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`) updated to match the new message verbatim (interpolated values are still only counts and an ISO timestamp — no new secret-bearing content).

**Test, and the pre-fix-fail verification the task required** (`server.test.ts:1028`, new): "a burst LARGER than MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT delivers exactly MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT live (documented residual) and fires the cap-hit warning" — seeds `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT + 50` (1050) events, entirely before the stream opens, timestamped in the future (the same seed-before-open discipline FIX 1's own sibling test uses, and for the identical reason — seeding after open lets the poll interval interleave with seeding and never actually land a true single-tick burst). Asserts (a) the oldest 50 (`evt-cap-0`..`49`) are never delivered while the newest 1000 (`evt-cap-50`..`1049`) all are — pinning the documented residual precisely, not just "some subset arrived" — and (b) `console.warn` was called, with a message naming both "per-tick cap" and `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT`'s own value. **Verified failing pre-fix** exactly as described in Problem 2 above, then verified passing post-fix; full dashboard suite (118 tests) and `packages/governance`'s own suite (218 tests, covering `redaction-lint-suppressions.ts`) both re-run clean afterward. `lint:redaction` re-run clean at 271 reviewed suppressions (unchanged count — one entry's snippet/reason updated in place, none added or removed).

**Scope fence honored — the genuine self-heal was NOT implemented, as instructed.** Making the live stream eventually catch up on a burst bigger than `MAX_EVENTS_LIMIT` needs an oldest-first / range query `EventLogStore.list` doesn't support today (`since`+`limit` only, newest-first only, no continuation token) — a real store/HTTP pagination-shape change, bigger than this correction's own scope. Left as a documented **FUTURE follow-up**, exactly as the original FIX 1 entry already flagged before this correction, now just honestly described rather than claimed already-solved.

**Files touched, nothing else:** `packages/dashboard/src/server.ts` (doc comment + `drainNewEventsSince`), `packages/dashboard/src/server.test.ts` (new test), `packages/governance/src/redaction-lint-suppressions.ts` (one suppression's snippet/reason updated to match), this file. The shutdown fix, the packaging, and every other Wave-2 fix are untouched — independently verified already, out of scope here.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && pnpm run check:tsconfig-refs && pnpm run build && pnpm run typecheck && pnpm run typecheck:tests && pnpm run lint:redaction && pnpm test`): **239 files / 2632 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** (the 2631-test baseline this same entry's own original session measured, +1 for the new test above). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes as every prior session, both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs, **271 reviewed suppressions** (unchanged from the count before this correction — one entry's snippet/reason updated in place, none added or removed). Both full run logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run. The new test (real `setInterval` polling, real fs I/O, a real HTTP round trip against a real `@aart/server`) was additionally run 5 more times in isolation beyond the two full-suite passes (7 total observed passes) — clean every time, zero flakiness, matching this fix pass's own established discipline for timing-sensitive tests.

## 2026-07-13 — 0.10.0 beta release prep

### A68 — 0.10.0 beta release prep: version stamp 0.1.0 → 0.10.0 across the published surface, a missing `aart --version` implemented, npm Trusted Publishing workflow recreated, install docs switched to npm/npx, a real tarball built/packed/installed/smoke-tested end to end — nothing pushed, published, or tagged

John ratified 2026-07-13: "0.10.0 sounds good ship it please by tagging it." **Why 0.10.0, not 1.0.0:** npm's `@team-monet/aart` already has legacy versions `0.1.0`–`0.9.0` published (the OLD pre-rebuild `aa-runtime` CLI, dist-tag `latest` → `0.9.0`) — this repo's own internal version was still `0.1.0`, itself already-published-legacy, so it could never publish at or below `0.9.0`. `0.10.0` sorts after `0.9.0` under semver and becomes the new `latest`, finally making `npm install -g @team-monet/aart`/`npx @team-monet/aart` resolve to the real, rebuilt CLI. `1.0.0` stays reserved for the production-ready release. Worked on an isolated branch (`release/0.10.0` off `main` at `c1d4d72`, in a dedicated git worktree), committed incrementally, nothing pushed — Stig handles push + tag after review; John's own account does the actual `npm publish`. No `git push`, `npm publish`, or `git tag` was run at any point in this session.

**1. Version stamp — the grep, and every file it found.** `grep -rn "0\.1\.0"` across `*.json/*.ts/*.md/*.mjs/*.js/*.yml/*.yaml`, excluding `node_modules/.git/dist/.changeset`, returned ~354 hits. Sorted into four buckets, each verified by reading the actual surrounding code/doc, not assumed from the filename:

- **The published package version — changed:** `packages/cli/package.json:3` (`"version"`). `packages/mcp/src/mcp-stdio.ts:37` — the MCP server's advertised `serverInfo.version` (a hardcoded literal, matching this codebase's existing convention; no dynamic `package.json` read — see deviation 2 below for why that would silently break). Both confirmed via grep beforehand that no test pins the old literal string, so neither bump could break an assertion.
- **Docs that denote the package version, as a literal tarball filename or install command — changed:** `AUTHORING.md:134-135`, `TEST-DRIVE.md:29,36`, `DEPLOY.md:198,201` (`team-monet-aart-0.1.0.tgz` → `-0.10.0.tgz`, and the matching `npm install -g` lines). `packages/cli/CHANGELOG.md:3` — NOT edited in place (that would rewrite shipped history); a new `## 0.10.0` section was prepended above the existing `## 0.1.0` entry instead.
- **Internal `@aart/*` package.json "version" fields — deliberately left at `0.1.0`, 13 files** (`blocks-core`, `dashboard`, `engine`, `evidence`, `expr`, `familiarity-evals`, `governance`, `llm`, `mcp`, `registry`, `server`, `store`, `types`). Verified, not assumed: every one of these carries `"private": true` (checked all 13 directly), and `packages/cli/package.json`'s own `devDependencies` reference them via the `workspace:*` protocol (resolved by pnpm's local symlinking at install time, never a registry lookup) — never listed in `dependencies`, so npm never fetches a consumer's `devDependencies` when installing the published tarball. The packed tarball's own `package.json` (inspected directly, see §2) shows exactly this: `"@aart/mcp": "0.1.0"` etc. sitting inertly under `devDependencies`, present but never resolved by `npm install @team-monet/aart`. Bumping 13 files with zero effect on publish correctness would be gratuitous, per the brief's own instruction not to.
- **AART's own domain "version" fields — not touched, ~330 hits.** Workflow definition versions (`examples/**/*.workflow.json`, `*.yaml`), block-catalog/recipe versions (`packages/mcp/src/catalog.ts` ×24, `packages/mcp/src/recipes.ts` ×7), and hundreds of test fixtures across every package using `"0.1.0"` as an arbitrary workflow/block version. This is a distinct, first-class versioning concept AART itself defines for workflows and blocks, orthogonal to the npm package's own version — `packages/blocks-core/src/lib/define-block.ts:20` already says so explicitly in its own doc comment ("this package's own block-catalog version, distinct from AART's package version"), and `packages/store/src/conformance.ts:124` already uses `"0.10.0"` as a third arbitrary fixture value alongside `"0.1.0"`/`"0.2.0"` in a version-list-ordering test — proof this test suite already treats these as opaque sortable domain strings having nothing to do with the CLI's own release number. None of `AMENDMENTS.md`'s own ~40 historical `0.1.0` mentions were touched either — those are the permanent record of what was true in past sessions; rewriting them would falsify history.

**2. The publishable tarball, built and verified for real, not assumed.** `pnpm run build && pnpm run build:dashboard-launcher` (root) failed nothing; `pnpm --filter @team-monet/aart run build:publish` confirmed `@team-monet/aart@0.10.0` bundling the same 8-package external list every prior session has verified (`@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, `ajv`, `isolated-vm`, `js-yaml`, `playwright`, `yaml`, `zod` — no drift). `pnpm pack` in `packages/cli` produced `team-monet-aart-0.10.0.tgz`, 1,862,662 bytes. Independent `tar -tzf` (not `pnpm pack`'s own summary) listed all 59 entries: 56 under `dist/**` (including `dist/frontend/**` — the dashboard SPA, confirming the Wave 2/A67 FIX 3 packaging survives this version bump) plus `LICENSE`, `package.json`, `README.md` at the root. A leak-check grep for `src/`, `.env`, `secrets.json`, `node_modules/`, `*.test.*` inside the tarball listing returned zero matches. The extracted `package.json` confirmed `"version": "0.10.0"` and the internal `@aart/*` `workspace:*` specifiers resolved to inert `0.1.0` devDependencies exactly as reasoned in §1. `grep -c "0.10.0" dist/bin.js` found 3 occurrences in the bundled output, including `"aart", version: "0.10.0"` — the MCP server's `serverInfo` construction, transitively bundled into the CLI (since `aart mcp` starts it), confirming both version edits from §1 genuinely survive esbuild bundling into what ships.

Then a real global install, into a throwaway prefix, of nothing but that tarball:
```
$ npm install -g --prefix <scratch>/npm-prefix ./team-monet-aart-0.10.0.tgz
npm warn EBADENGINE Unsupported engine {package: 'isolated-vm@7.0.0', required: {node: '>=26.0.0'}, current: {node: 'v22.22.2', ...}}
changed 105 packages in 30s
```
(the exact, previously-documented, harmless warning — nothing new). From that installed binary, on `PATH`:
```
$ aart --version
0.10.0
$ aart -v
0.10.0
$ aart --help   # exit 0, real USAGE block
$ aart environment register x --trust-mode dev --root <scratch>/aart-smoke-root
{"ok": true, "environment": {"id": "env_54bc...", "name": "x", "config": {"trustMode": "dev"}}}
$ aart environment list --root <scratch>/aart-smoke-root
{"ok": true, "environments": [{"id": "env_54bc...", "name": "x", "config": {"trustMode": "dev"}}]}
```
`find <scratch>/npm-prefix/lib/node_modules/@team-monet/aart -iname "@aart*"` — zero matches, confirming no private workspace package leaked into the real installed tree. This is genuine end-to-end proof the 0.10.0 tarball is installable and runnable as a real global package, not just that it builds.

**3. `aart --version` did not exist — implemented, not just re-numbered.** The brief's own step 2 requires `aart --version` to print `0.10.0`; reading `packages/cli/src/cli.ts`'s `run()` switch and `packages/cli/src/bin.ts` directly showed no `--version`/`-v` case anywhere, and this repo's own `AMENDMENTS.md` A54 entry already recorded exactly this gap in passing ("`aart --version` isn't a real command in this CLI's surface — the `USAGE` string has no such flag"). Rather than silently substituting a different read-only command (as A54's own session did for an unrelated proof) or stopping the whole release over one missing flag, implemented it as a small, precedented addition: a `VERSION` constant beside `USAGE` in `cli.ts`, a `run()` case mirroring the existing `--help`/`-h`/`help` defense-in-depth shape exactly, and a `bin.ts` short-circuit (plain stdout, exit 0, no store construction) mirroring `--help`'s real-process handling exactly. Two new tests mirror the existing `--help`/`-h`/`help` describe block. Flagged as a deviation below, not silently folded in as if it were always there.

**4. `packages/cli/LICENSE` added.** The tarball-content requirement (§2) expects a shipped `LICENSE`; `packages/cli/` had no `LICENSE` file of its own (only a `"license": "Apache-2.0"` field in `package.json`) — npm's automatic "always include LICENSE" packing behavior only reaches into the package's own directory, not up to the monorepo root, so nothing would have been picked up. ### A69 — 0.10.0 container publishing: GHCR multi-arch workflow, the A67 Dockerfile frontend-dir gap closed, worker `/health` version fixed — same `v0.10.0` tag ships both npm and the image

John ratified 2026-07-13 shipping 0.10.0 by tag; separately, "we also need to publish containers." Worked on the same isolated branch (`release/0.10.0` off `main` at `c1d4d72`, in a dedicated git worktree) A68 already prepared — tip `d193d05` at the start of this session, untouched/unclobbered — committed incrementally, nothing pushed. Goal: `docker pull ghcr.io/team-monet/aart` / `docker compose up` against a published image becomes the documented server-deployment path, instead of `--build` from a clone, riding the exact same `v0.10.0` tag the npm publish already rides.

**1. Dockerfile verified against the A67 dashboard-packaging change, and fixed — real `docker build`/`docker run`, not review-only.** A67 FIX 3 (Wave 2 fix pass) moved where the dashboard SPA actually ships: `deploy/build-dashboard-launcher.mjs` now copies `packages/dashboard/dist/frontend` to `packages/cli/dist/frontend`, a direct sibling of the bundled `serve-dashboard.mjs`. The Dockerfile's `runtime-base` stage still set `AART_DASHBOARD_FRONTEND_DIR=/app/packages/dashboard/dist/frontend` — the PRE-A67 location. Read `@aart/dashboard`'s `getFrontendDir()` (`packages/dashboard/src/server.ts`) directly before concluding anything: it checks this env var FIRST, and only falls through to an `__dirname`-relative guess (whose first candidate, `path.join(__dirname, "frontend")`, is exactly the NEW A67 copy location) if the var is unset or its target has no `index.html`. Then checked whether the old path is still physically present in the built image: `pruned`'s `COPY --from=pruned /workspace /app` preserves the entire built workspace, and nothing in `build-dashboard-launcher.mjs` deletes the source it copies FROM (only `rmSync(FRONTEND_DEST, ...)` on the destination) — so `packages/dashboard/dist/frontend` genuinely still exists in the image too. Conclusion, confirmed rather than assumed: the old env var value was **not broken**, just stale/redundant — two copies of the same SPA assets now ship, and the env var pointed at the (now non-canonical) one. Fixed by repointing it to `/app/packages/cli/dist/frontend`, kept explicit (not deleted in favor of the fallback guess) to match the exact same belt-and-braces precedent `aart watch`'s own `buildDashboardEnv` (`packages/cli/src/commands/watch.ts`) already established for this identical var.

Verified for real, both before deciding this was low-risk enough to fix-and-verify-once (not build twice) and after landing the fix: `docker build --target runtime -t aart:verify-runtime .` (multi-stage, `node:22-bookworm-slim`, ~1.65GB) succeeded clean. Ran all three roles as real containers on a dedicated bridge network + shared named volume (mirroring `docker-compose.yml`'s own topology):

```
$ curl http://localhost:18080/health
{"status":"ok"}
$ curl http://localhost:18787/health
{"status":"ok","claimedRuns":0,"uptime":10.556,"version":"0.10.0"}
$ curl http://localhost:14000/
<!doctype html>
<html lang="en" class="dark">
  <head>
    ...
    <script type="module" crossorigin src="/assets/index-xcyHcir8.js"></script>
    <link rel="stylesheet" crossorigin href="/assets/index-DMZaAw8T.css">
  ...
$ curl -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" http://localhost:14000/assets/index-xcyHcir8.js
200
```

Server `/health` 200; worker `/health` 200 (see §3 below for that `"version":"0.10.0"` field specifically); dashboard `GET /` 200 with the real Vite SPA (not a 404 — the exact failure mode A67 itself was closing), confirmed by also fetching the hashed JS asset the HTML references, not just the HTML shell. `docker logs aart-verify-dashboard` showed no `"AART_DASHBOARD_FRONTEND_DIR is set... but no index.html was found there"` fallback warning (`server.ts`'s own A52 warning path) — the override resolved cleanly on the first try. All verification containers/network/volume/image removed afterward (`docker rm -f`/`network rm`/`volume rm`/`rmi`) — nothing left running.

**2. Container publish workflow — a separate file, not a second job in `publish.yml`.** `.github/workflows/publish-containers.yml`, triggered on the identical `push: tags: ['v*']` `publish.yml` already uses. Separate file rather than a second job in that same workflow: `publish.yml`'s whole design is npm OIDC trusted publishing (`permissions: id-token: write`, deliberately no package-registry grant); this workflow's design is GHCR (`permissions: packages: write`, deliberately no `id-token`). One file per distinct permission surface keeps each minimal and lets either publish path be independently re-run from the Actions tab without touching the other's job history — a failed container push doesn't retry or get entangled with a successful npm publish, or vice versa.

Auth is the built-in, ephemeral `GITHUB_TOKEN` via `docker/login-action` against `ghcr.io` — no external secret, matching the brief's own requirement and this repo's now-established "no long-lived credential in a workflow file" convention (`publish.yml`'s own OIDC choice, same underlying discipline). `docker/setup-qemu-action` + `docker/setup-buildx-action` + `docker/build-push-action@v6` build the `runtime` target — the SAME target `docker-compose.yml`'s three services already run, and this `Dockerfile`'s own last/default stage — for `linux/amd64,linux/arm64`. Tags computed by `docker/metadata-action`: `type=semver,pattern={{version}}` (validates the pushed tag is well-formed semver AND strips the leading `v` — `v0.10.0` → `0.10.0`, matching `package.json`'s own no-`v`-prefix convention) plus an unconditional `type=raw,value=latest`, with `flavor: latest=false` added specifically to disable `metadata-action`'s own separate conditional auto-latest heuristic — this repo's convention is every tagged release becomes `latest`, deterministically, not conditionally on a highest-version comparison the action would otherwise make on its own. `provenance: true`/`sbom: true` on `build-push-action` — both single boolean flags, not hand-rolled attestation generation, so "provenance if easy" is satisfied without over-engineering.

`runtime-browser` (Playwright/Chromium, ~3GB vs ~1.2GB, AMENDMENTS.md A49) is **not** published by this workflow — a deliberate call, noted in-file, not a silent omission. Reasoning: (a) it would double this job's already-multi-arch-under-QEMU build/push time and registry storage for every tag, (b) `docker-compose.yml`'s three services default to `runtime`, never `runtime-browser`, so the core "published image the compose file actually pulls" goal is fully satisfied by `runtime` alone, (c) the Dockerfile's own established convention already treats `runtime-browser` as an explicit `--target` opt-in, never an accidental default — this workflow mirrors that same default rather than inventing a new one for the published-image path specifically. The in-file comment documents the extension: a second `build-push-action` step, `target: runtime-browser`, a `-browser` tag suffix, same platforms.

Header comment states the one manual, one-time prerequisite explicitly (the brief's own required "HEADER COMMENT" item): a new GHCR package is created **private** by default on its first push, regardless of the parent repo's own visibility — anonymous `docker pull ghcr.io/team-monet/aart` 401s/404s until a repo admin flips it once: GitHub → the owning org/user → Packages → `aart` → Package settings → Change visibility → Public. This workflow cannot do that step itself (a one-time package-level setting, not a per-run action the granted `GITHUB_TOKEN` can perform).

`timeout-minutes: 45` — generous, not tuned against a single sample: AMENDMENTS.md A49 already found `isolated-vm` compiling from source (a real g++ build) rather than using its own bundled prebuild on at least one architecture running natively; under QEMU emulation for the non-native leg of a true multi-arch build, that compile (and the rest of the from-scratch `pnpm install`/`tsc -b`/esbuild-bundle sequence) is expected to run meaningfully slower than this session's own native-only local verification build did.

**3. `packages/server/src/worker/health.ts`'s `resolveVersion()` — the exact gap A68 deviation 6 flagged, fixed here, superseding the standalone `task_eefddf71` it spawned.** Read `packages/cli/src/cli.ts`'s own `VERSION` constant and its doc comment directly (not guessed): a hardcoded string literal, `"0.10.0"`, deliberately NOT read from `package.json` at runtime, because `packages/cli`'s esbuild bundle (`build:publish`) inlines the whole CLI into a single `dist/bin.js`, and a relative-path/`__dirname`-based `package.json` read does not survive that bundling — that constant's own comment names `health.ts`'s `resolveVersion` as "a real instance of exactly this failure mode." Confirmed the mechanism: `resolveVersion()` did `readFileSync(join(here, "..", "..", "package.json"))`, correct only for an unbundled `packages/server/dist/worker/health.js` layout; once bundled into `packages/cli/dist/bin.js`, that walk lands two directories above wherever the bundle physically runs from — in a real global npm install, that's inside `node_modules/@team-monet/`, where no `package.json` exists at all, so the read throws, is caught, and falls through to the function's own hardcoded `"0.0.0"` catch-block fallback. `claimedRuns`/`uptime`/`status` were unaffected — this was cosmetic (a wrong version string), not a functional break, which is exactly why it shipped unnoticed through however many prior sessions.

Applied the SAME mechanism `cli.ts` uses, not a different one: removed `resolveVersion()` and its now-dead imports (`readFileSync`/`fileURLToPath`/`dirname`/`join` — none of node:fs/node:url/node:path's other exports were used elsewhere in this file), replaced with an exported hardcoded constant, `PUBLISHED_CLI_VERSION = "0.10.0"`, used directly in the `/health` response payload. Named deliberately NOT `VERSION` (which would read as this package's own version — `@aart/server`'s `package.json` stays frozen at `"0.1.0"` by A68 §1's own deliberate, unrelated choice, since it's one of 13 private, never-independently-published internal packages) — `PUBLISHED_CLI_VERSION` states plainly which version this actually is: the published `@team-monet/aart` CLI's, because `aart worker` IS that same CLI, just a different subcommand of the identical `dist/bin.js`.

**Two independent hardcoded literals, not one shared constant — a considered tradeoff, not an oversight.** `@aart/server` cannot import from `@aart/cli` (the CLI depends on the server package, never the reverse — this repo's own package layering). The alternative considered — threading the real value through `WorkerConfig`/`startWorker` from `packages/cli`'s own `VERSION`, the same dependency-injection shape this codebase already uses pervasively for `healthPort`/`deployToken`/`environmentId`/etc. — would produce a genuine single source of truth (no drift risk) but touches `WorkerConfig`, `startWorker`, `workerCommand`, and every test constructing a worker. Rejected as disproportionate to a brief explicitly scoped as "a SMALL release-quality fix": the two-literal approach is a 2-file diff, exactly mirrors the precedent the brief pointed at, and the drift risk it accepts (bump `cli.ts`'s `VERSION` and forget `health.ts`'s `PUBLISHED_CLI_VERSION`, or vice versa) is the identical, already-accepted trade-off `cli.ts`'s own `VERSION` doc comment documents for ITS relationship to `package.json` — not a new risk class, and `health.ts`'s own doc comment cross-references `cli.ts`'s `VERSION` explicitly so a future release-bump session greps straight to both.

`worker.test.ts`'s existing `GET /health` test (`describe("GET /health ...")`) asserted only `expect(typeof body.version).toBe("string")` — satisfied equally by the broken `"0.0.0"` fallback, which is precisely how this shipped undetected through every prior session's own from-clean gate run. Tightened to `expect(body.version).toBe(PUBLISHED_CLI_VERSION)`, importing that constant from `./health.js` rather than re-hardcoding the literal a second time in the test (mirroring `cli.test.ts`'s own `--version`/`-v` test, A68 §3, which imports and compares against `VERSION` the same way rather than a bare string). Verified against the real container in §1 above: `GET /health` → `"version":"0.10.0"`, not `"0.0.0"`.

**Gates, from clean, run TWICE in full** (`pnpm run clean && pnpm run check:tsconfig-refs && pnpm run build && pnpm run typecheck && pnpm run typecheck:tests && pnpm run lint:redaction && pnpm test`) — this worktree had never had `pnpm install` run in it (a fresh worktree checkout has no `node_modules`); `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` first (511 packages, clean, only the expected pre-build "governance-lint bin not found yet" warnings — resolved by the `build` step immediately after, same as any fresh clone). Both full runs happened with §1–§3's code/config changes already in the working tree (Dockerfile, the new workflow file, `health.ts`/`worker.test.ts` — all landed before either run started); §4's own edits (`docker-compose.yml`, `DEPLOY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`) followed shortly after, interleaved with the Docker container verification in §1 — immaterial to what these gates check either way, since none of `tsc`/`vitest`/the redaction linter (which scans package `src/` dirs only) reads a `.yml`/`.md` file at all; §3's `health.ts`/`worker.test.ts` is the only part of this entire diff these specific gate commands actually exercise, and it was present for both runs. Result: **239 test files / 2634 tests, all green, byte-for-byte identical across both complete from-clean runs** — the exact same count A68 itself recorded, confirming this session's `health.ts` test edit changed an assertion, not the test count (one existing `it()` tightened, none added/removed). `check:tsconfig-refs`: OK, the same 5 non-blocking informational notes both runs. `lint:redaction`: clean both runs, **271 reviewed suppressions** — unchanged from A67/A68, confirming this session touched nothing redaction-lint-relevant. Both full run logs swept for hidden failures (`grep -niE "error TS|FAIL|✗|UnhandledRejection|ELIFECYCLE"`) — zero matches either run. Run 1: 17.60s test duration. Run 2: 14.60s. The Docker build/run verification in §1 was run separately (outside these two gate runs, using its own from-scratch install inside the container per the `Dockerfile`'s own design), against the exact committed tree these two gate runs verified.

**4. `docker-compose.yml` + `DEPLOY.md` pointed at the published image, `DEPLOY.md`'s existing A68 edits read first and left untouched.** `docker-compose.yml`: each of the three services (`server`/`worker`/`dashboard`) now defaults to `image: ghcr.io/team-monet/aart:0.10.0` — pinned to the exact version, not `:latest`, so this file's own "SHAPE to adapt, not a drop-in prod config" framing (its own header comment, unchanged) doesn't let an unrelated `docker compose pull` silently drift a running deployment out from under an operator; `DEPLOY.md`'s "Upgrade procedure" is where a version bump belongs, deliberately.
